# Digest 20261002-190752

Date: 2026-10-02. **Backfill run for 2025** (`since: 2025-01-01`, `until: 2025-12-31`), requested by the user, limited to the EGFR-focused searches: egfr-nsclc, nsclc-resistance-nextgen, lung-top-journals. The broad searches (lung-key-evidence, lung-nonpharm), the preprint feeds (which cannot be backfilled) and approvals-access were not run. The regular date window is unaffected (`--until` never moves it).

## Source errors and gaps

- **No source errors and no truncation.** Long PubMed windows were split into date slices (`slices` in `state/runs.jsonl`) and every match was retrieved.
- **Lighter backfill triage** (CLAUDE.md, step 4): every item has a relevance, but low items have no reason and medium reasons are one line. Triage was done in parallel batches from titles, publication types and abstracts or registry fields.
- ClinicalTrials.gov "results posted" items carry no outcome numbers in the monitor record; read the posted results on the registry before citing findings.
- Some 2025 papers are already superseded by 2026 updates found in earlier runs (e.g. MARIPOSA and FLAURA2 survival data).

## High

### egfr-nsclc

- **Safety, Pharmacokinetics, and Efficacy of Asandeutertinib in Advanced EGFR-mutated NSCLC: A Phase 1 Dose-Escalation and Dose-Expansion Study.**: Phase 1 dose-escalation/expansion study (n=105) of TY-9591 (asandeutertinib), a deuterated osimertinib derivative. No DLTs and MTD not reached; grade >=3 TRAEs in 30.5%. In the first-line expansion cohort (n=79), confirmed ORR was 85.9% and median PFS 21.5 months (L858R subgroup n=36: ORR 86.1%, PFS 19.3 months). Single-arm, uncontrolled phase 1 data; efficacy needs confirmation against osimertinib in a randomised trial.
  Han B, Liu J, Wu L et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39929334/
- **Rezivertinib versus gefitinib as first-line therapy for patients with EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer (REZOR): a multicentre, double-blind, randomised, phase 3 study.**: Double-blind phase 3 trial in 50 Chinese hospitals randomising 369 patients with ex19del/L858R NSCLC to the third-generation TKI rezivertinib or gefitinib. Median PFS by masked central review was 19.3 vs 9.6 months (HR 0.48, 95% CI 0.36-0.63), with similar grade >=3 TRAE rates (23% in both arms). Adds another third-generation TKI with phase 3 superiority over a first-generation comparator. The comparator is gefitinib rather than osimertinib, the population is Chinese only, and OS data are not yet mature.
  Shi Y, Guo Y, Li X et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39914443/
- **First-Line Mobocertinib Versus Platinum-Based Chemotherapy in Patients With EGFR Exon 20 Insertion-Positive Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer in the Phase III EXCLAIM-2 Trial.**: Open-label phase 3 trial (n=354) of first-line mobocertinib vs platinum-pemetrexed in EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC. At interim analysis, median PFS by central review was 9.6 months in both arms (HR 1.04), crossing the futility boundary; ORR was 32% vs 30%, with more grade >=3 diarrhoea on mobocertinib (20% vs 1%). A negative trial that removes mobocertinib as a first-line option for exon 20 insertions. Open-label design, and the result is from an interim analysis.
  Jänne PA, Wang BC, Cho BC et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39879577/
- **Durvalumab, Tremelimumab, and Platinum Chemotherapy in EGFR Mutation-Positive NSCLC: An Open-Label Phase 2 Trial (ILLUMINATE).**: Open-label phase 2 trial (n=100, Australia/Taiwan) of durvalumab, tremelimumab and platinum-pemetrexed after EGFR-TKI progression, in T790M-negative and T790M-positive cohorts. ORR was 31% (T790M-negative) and 21% (T790M-positive), with median PFS of 6.5 and 4.9 months. The authors judge the activity modest, which does not support adding dual checkpoint blockade after TKI failure. Single-arm with no chemotherapy-alone control, and PD-L1 subgroups are very small.
  Lee CK, Liao BC, Subramaniam S et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39877028/
- **Preventing Infusion-Related Reactions With Intravenous Amivantamab-Results From SKIPPirr, a Phase 2 Study: A Brief Report.**: Phase 2 study (n=68 across four prophylaxis cohorts) in patients receiving IV amivantamab plus lazertinib after osimertinib and platinum chemotherapy. Oral dexamethasone 8 mg twice daily on days -2 to 1 cut cycle 1 day 1 infusion-related reactions to 22.5% (9/40), against a historical 67.4%. This is a simple premedication that can be used in practice with IV amivantamab. Comparison is with historical data rather than a randomised control, and the cohort is small.
  Spira AI, Paz-Ares L, Han JY et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39864547/
- **Neratinib Efficacy in Patients With EGFR Exon 18-Mutant Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer: Findings From the SUMMIT Basket Trial.**: Phase 2 basket-trial cohort of neratinib in EGFR exon 18-mutant NSCLC (n=31; 24 pretreated with a TKI). ORR was 32.3% and median PFS 5.75 months; six patients with G719X mutations had responses lasting more than 10 months, and 2 of 7 with CNS metastases responded. Provides some data for a rare mutation subgroup with few options after TKI failure. Very small single-arm cohort.
  Goldman JW, Bueno AM, Dooms C et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39828466/
- **Results from a phase Ib study of telisotuzumab vedotin in combination with osimertinib in patients with c-Met protein-overexpressing, EGFR-mutated locally advanced/metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) after progression on prior osimertinib.**: Phase I/Ib study (n=38) of the c-Met ADC telisotuzumab vedotin plus osimertinib in c-Met-overexpressing EGFR-mutant NSCLC after osimertinib progression. ORR by central review was 50.0% and median PFS 7.4 months; the main toxicities were peripheral sensory neuropathy (50%) and oedema (32%), with no DLTs. Supports a MET-protein-directed ADC strategy after osimertinib. Small, single-arm study with median follow-up of only 7.4 months.
  Horinouchi H, Cho BC, Camidge DR et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39805351/
- **[Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Management of Brain Metastases from Non-small Cell Lung Cancer with Actionable Gene Alterations in China (2025 Edition)].**: Chinese multidisciplinary clinical practice guideline (2025 edition) for brain metastases in driver-positive NSCLC, including EGFR. It covers diagnosis, timing of intervention, and systemic and local treatment by driver type. Relevant as a CNS-management guideline update. It is a Chinese-language national guideline, and the abstract gives no specific recommendations or evidence grading.
  Lung Cancer Medical Education Committee of Chinese Medical Education Association, Brain Metastasis Collaboration Group, Youth Specialists Committee of Lung Cancer of Beijing Medical Reward Foundation. *Zhongguo Fei Ai Za Zhi*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39763097/
- **Datopotamab Deruxtecan in Advanced or Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Actionable Genomic Alterations: Results From the Phase II TROPION-Lung05 Study.**: Phase 2 single-arm trial (n=137; 56.9% EGFR-mutant, 71.5% with >=3 prior lines) of the TROP2 ADC Dato-DXd after targeted therapy and platinum chemotherapy. Confirmed ORR was 35.8% overall and 43.6% in EGFR-mutant patients, with a median DOR of 7.0 months; grade >=3 TRAEs occurred in 28.5%, stomatitis in 56.2% (any grade), and adjudicated ILD in 3.6% (one fatal). Key evidence for an ADC option in EGFR-mutant disease after osimertinib. Single-arm with no comparator, in a heavily pretreated population.
  Sands J, Ahn MJ, Lisberg A et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39761483/
- **Consensus on the lung cancer management after third-generation EGFR-TKI resistance.**: Asia-Pacific expert consensus on managing NSCLC after resistance to third-generation EGFR-TKIs. It gives recommendations on classifying progression patterns, testing at resistance, and post-resistance treatment strategies. Directly relevant to sequencing after osimertinib. It is an expert consensus rather than a systematic evidence review, and the abstract does not list the specific recommendations.
  Zhou Q, Zhao H, Lu S et al. *Lancet Reg Health West Pac*, 2024. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39759798/
- **Monitoring of Circulating Tumor DNA and Indication of De-Escalation Adjuvant Targeted Therapy for EGFR-Mutated NSCLC After Complete Resection.**: Observational study of 71 patients with resected stage I-III EGFR-mutant NSCLC who had longitudinal tumour-informed ctDNA-MRD testing (375 samples). Longitudinal MRD had 86.2% sensitivity and 88.2% negative predictive value. In 22 patients who stopped adjuvant TKI because MRD was undetectable, 2-year DFS was 80.2%, and 5 who recurred off drug were stable on retreatment. An early signal for MRD-guided de-escalation of adjuvant TKIs. Small, non-randomised, single-assay study with short follow-up.
  Dong S, Yan B, Liu SY et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39758595/
- **A phase II trial of anlotinib plus EGFR-TKIs in advanced non-small cell lung cancer with gradual, oligo, or potential progression after EGFR-TKIs treatment (CTONG-1803/ALTER-L001).**: Single-arm multicentre phase 2 (n=120) adding anlotinib to continued EGFR-TKI in patients with gradual, oligo or potential progression. Median PFS was 9.1 months (10.3 months after first-line third-generation TKI, n=32); ORR was 6.7% and DCR 87.5%, with grade >=3 TEAEs in 52.5%. Suggests continuing the TKI with an added antiangiogenic for slow progression. No control arm, so the benefit over continuing the TKI alone or switching treatment is unproven; few patients had osimertinib first line.
  Chen HJ, Tu HY, Hu Y et al. *J Hematol Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39757186/
- **Amivantamab Plus Lazertinib in Patients With EGFR-Mutant NSCLC After Progression on Osimertinib and Platinum-Based Chemotherapy: Results From CHRYSALIS-2 Cohort A.**: Phase 1/2 cohort (n=162) of amivantamab plus lazertinib after osimertinib and platinum chemotherapy. ORR by central review was 35% (investigator 28%), with median DOR 8.3 months, PFS 4.5 months and OS 14.8 months; responses occurred with and without EGFR/MET-dependent resistance. Defines the activity of this combination in a heavily pretreated post-osimertinib population. Single-arm; toxicity is substantial (rash 81%, infusion reactions 68%), and CNS data are limited to seven patients.
  Besse B, Goto K, Wang Y et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39755170/
- **A Prospective Phase II Trial of First-Line Osimertinib for Patients With EGFR Mutation-Positive NSCLC and Poor Performance Status (OPEN/TORG2040).**: Prospective phase 2 trial (n=30, PS 2-4, median age 75) of first-line osimertinib. ORR was 63.3%, PS improved in 63.3%, and median PFS and OS were 8.0 and 25.4 months. However, 20% developed ILD and 26.7% had serious AEs leading to discontinuation. Supports osimertinib in frail patients but highlights the ILD risk. Small single-arm Japanese study.
  Fukui T, Mamesaya N, Takahashi T et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39755169/
- **Neoadjuvant Aumolertinib for unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant non-small cell lung cancer: a single-arm phase II trial.**: Single-arm phase 2 (n=51 ITT) of neoadjuvant aumolertinib in unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC. ORR was 70.6% and 45.1% converted to resection (R0 100%, MPR 21.7%, pCR 13.0%), with 1- and 2-year EFS of 88.2% and 58.8% and grade 3/4 TRAEs in 9.8%. Supports TKI-based conversion strategies in locally advanced disease. Single-arm with no comparison to chemoradiotherapy followed by osimertinib (the LAURA approach); OS is immature.
  Bian D, Ji S, Liu Y et al. *Nat Commun*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40169605/
- **Randomized Phase III Study of EGFR Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor and Intercalated Platinum-Doublet Chemotherapy for Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Harboring EGFR Mutation.**: Japanese open-label phase 3 (n=501) of first-line EGFR-TKI (gefitinib, later osimertinib) alone vs with intercalated cisplatin-pemetrexed. PFS improved (18.0 vs 12.0 months, HR 0.762), but OS did not (median 48.0 months in both arms, HR 0.985). A negative OS result relevant to the debate on adding chemotherapy to first-line TKI. The design changed mid-trial (gefitinib cohort n=308, osimertinib cohort n=193), and the intercalated schedule differs from FLAURA2.
  Kanda S, Niho S, Kurata T et al. *Clin Cancer Res*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40162917/
- **Molecular residual disease analysis of adjuvant osimertinib in resected EGFR-mutated stage IB-IIIA non-small-cell lung cancer.**: Exploratory post hoc tumour-informed ctDNA-MRD analysis of 220 ADAURA patients. MRD preceded imaging DFS events by a median of 4.7 months; 36-month DFS/MRD event-free rate was 86% with osimertinib vs 36% with placebo (HR 0.23). Most events on the osimertinib arm (68%) occurred after stopping treatment. Suggests MRD could identify patients who need longer adjuvant therapy. Post hoc subset analysis, and MRD-guided duration still needs prospective confirmation.
  Herbst RS, John T, Grohé C et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40097663/
- **Aumolertinib plus chemotherapy as first-line treatment for advanced NSCLC with EGFR exon 19 deletion or exon 21 L858R: a phase II trial.**: Single-arm phase 2 (n=34) of first-line aumolertinib plus pemetrexed-carboplatin. Median PFS was 28.0 months and ORR 91.2%; 78.6% cleared EGFR ctDNA after 2-4 cycles, and clearance was associated with better outcomes. Grade >=3 neutropenia occurred in 64.7%. Adds to the FLAURA2-type TKI-plus-chemotherapy evidence. Small single-arm study; OS is immature.
  Li Y, Li C, Zhao X et al. *Oncologist*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40088185/
- **A randomised non-comparative phase II study of atezolizumab, bevacizumab and chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with acquired resistance - The ETOP 15-19 ABC-lung trial.**: European randomised non-comparative phase 2 (n=95) of atezolizumab-bevacizumab with carboplatin-paclitaxel or pemetrexed after EGFR-TKI resistance. Neither arm met the 1-year PFS target: median PFS was 6.4 and 7.6 months, and OS 15.4 and 15.6 months. A negative result for IMpower150-like regimens after TKI failure. Non-comparative design with no standard-chemotherapy control.
  Soo RA, Vervita K, Früh M et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40023017/
- **Basal-shift transformation leads to EGFR therapy-resistance in human lung adenocarcinoma.**: Biobank of patient-derived EGFR-mutant lung cancer organoids, including TKI-pretreated cases, profiled with single-cell and molecular analyses. A subgroup of TKI-resistant organoids without known genetic resistance lesions showed a 'basal-shift' phenotype (mixed adenocarcinoma/squamous gene expression); NKX2-1 knockout reproduced the shift and TKI resistance, and basal-shift tumours often had CDKN2A/B loss and were sensitive to CDK4/6 inhibitors. This identifies a non-genetic resistance mechanism with a candidate treatment, but evidence is preclinical (organoids) and the abstract gives no clinical validation or frequency in patients.
  Shinozaki T, Togasaki K, Hamamoto J et al. *Nat Commun*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40350470/
- **Lazertinib for Patients with NSCLC Harboring Uncommon EGFR Mutations: A Phase II Multicenter Trial.**: Single-arm multicentre phase II trial of lazertinib 240 mg in advanced NSCLC with uncommon EGFR mutations other than exon 20 insertions (n=36). ORR was 50.0% (95% CI 34.5-65.5%), meeting the primary endpoint; median PFS 10.8 months and median DoR 15.1 months, with ORR 61% and median PFS 20.3 months for G719X; grade 3+ adverse events occurred in 33.3%. Supports lazertinib as an option for uncommon EGFR mutations, but the trial is small, single-arm and without a comparator.
  Park S, Ahn HK, Lee S et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40350080/
- **Amivantamab plus lazertinib versus osimertinib as first-line treatment in EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer: MARIPOSA Asian subset.**: Subgroup analysis of the 629 Asian participants in the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial of first-line amivantamab plus lazertinib versus osimertinib in common EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. At 22.5 months median follow-up, median PFS was 27.5 vs 18.3 months (HR 0.65; P<0.001), ORR 88% vs 85%, with favourable OS trends and safety similar to the overall population. Confirms that the MARIPOSA PFS benefit holds in Asian patients, but this is a subgroup analysis and OS data are immature (trends only in this report).
  Cho BC, Hayashi H, Lee JS et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40300278/
- **Pragmatic Randomized Study of Afatinib Versus Chemotherapy for Patients With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Uncommon Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Mutations: ACHILLES/TORG1834.**: ACHILLES/TORG1834: randomised, open-label trial at 51 Japanese institutions (n=109, 2:1) comparing afatinib with platinum-pemetrexed in treatment-naive nonsquamous NSCLC with sensitising uncommon EGFR mutations (excluding exon 20 insertions and T790M). The trial stopped early at interim analysis; median PFS was 10.6 vs 5.7 months (HR 0.421, 95% CI 0.251-0.706; P=.0010), with afatinib ORR 61.7% overall and similar across major uncommon, compound and other mutations. First randomised evidence supporting an EGFR TKI over chemotherapy for uncommon mutations; limitations are early stopping, open-label design, a Japanese-only population, immature OS and no comparison with osimertinib or other newer TKIs.
  Miura S, Tanaka H, Misumi T et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40239133/
- **Sacituzumab tirumotecan in advanced non-small-cell lung cancer with or without EGFR mutations: phase 1/2 and phase 2 trials.**: Reports the TROP2 ADC sacituzumab tirumotecan in previously treated advanced NSCLC from the phase 1/2 KL264-01 study (n=43, EGFR-wild-type and EGFR-mutant) and the phase 2 SKB264-II-08 study (n=64, EGFR-mutant). In KL264-01 confirmed ORR was 40% (median PFS 6.2 months), rising to 55% with median PFS 11.1 months in the post-hoc EGFR-mutant subset (n=22); SKB264-II-08 found ORR 34% and median PFS 9.3 months in EGFR-mutant disease, with mainly haematological toxicity and ILD in 1%. Supports sac-TMT as a post-TKI option in EGFR-mutant NSCLC, which phase 3 trials are now testing; the data are single-arm and the EGFR-mutant signal in KL264-01 comes from a post-hoc subgroup.
  Zhao S, Cheng Y, Wang Q et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40210967/
- **PMDA regulatory update on approval and revision of the precautions for use of anticancer drugs; approval of amivantamab plus lazertinib for non-small cell lung cancer, durvalumab for small cell lung cancer, tislelizumab for esophageal cancer, tisotumab vedotin for cervical cancer, ivosidenib for leukemia, and venetoclax for lymphoma in Japan.**: Regulatory update from Japan's PMDA listing recent anticancer drug approvals, including amivantamab plus lazertinib for NSCLC (no abstract available). It records a new approval of an EGFR-directed combination in Japan. The exact indication wording and approval date are not given in the available text.
  Matsumura N, Mandai M. *Int J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40214878/
- **High-Dose Aumolertinib for Untreated EGFR-Variant Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Brain Metastases: The ACHIEVE Phase 2 Nonrandomized Clinical Trial.**: ACHIEVE: single-arm phase 2 trial at 10 Chinese centres of high-dose aumolertinib (165 mg daily) in untreated EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases (n=63; 49 with measurable brain lesions). At 28.8 months median follow-up, 12-month PFS was 62.1%, median PFS 20.5 months, 12-month intracranial PFS 76.8%, and systemic/intracranial ORR 88.9%/82.5%; grade 3-4 CPK rise occurred in 27%, and early plasma ctDNA clearance was associated with longer PFS (HR 0.14). Adds CNS efficacy data for dose-intensified third-generation TKI, but it is nonrandomised, has no standard-dose comparator, and median OS is not yet reached.
  Li H, Chen K, Gong L et al. *JAMA Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40569623/
- **Management of Patients With Early-Stage Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: An American College of Chest Physicians Clinical Practice Guideline.**: American College of Chest Physicians guideline on primary treatment of stage I-II NSCLC, built on a systematic review of 578 studies with GRADE and modified Delphi, producing 17 recommendations. Among them: surgery remains standard for stage I; minimally invasive approaches are preferred; sublobar resection is acceptable for peripheral tumours of 2 cm or less; adjuvant chemotherapy plus checkpoint inhibitors improves OS in resected stage II; and adjuvant targeted therapy improves OS in resected stage IB (3 cm or larger) to II EGFR-mutant disease. A major guideline that endorses adjuvant EGFR-targeted therapy; the abstract does not give the strength or evidence grade of each recommendation.
  Howington J, Souter LH, Arenberg D et al. *Chest*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40562304/
- **Targeting both wild-type EGFR and its drug-resistant mutants with erlotinib-aptamer conjugates.**: Medicinal-chemistry study building a lysosome-targeting chimera (LYTAC) from erlotinib linked to an IGF2R aptamer. The lead degrader LY-dE#5 degraded wild-type EGFR and the 19del, L858R/T790M and triple C797S mutants, outperformed osimertinib in the reported assays, and suppressed EGFR-driven tumour growth in vitro and in vivo. A pan-mutant EGFR degrader with in vivo activity is relevant to C797S-mediated resistance, but the evidence is preclinical only and the abstract gives no quantitative in vivo results.
  Li Y, Song J, Ge R et al. *Eur J Med Chem*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40554308/
- **Efficacy and safety of limertinib versus gefitinib as first-line treatment for locally advanced or metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer with EGFR-sensitising mutation: a randomised, double-blind, double-dummy, phase 3 trial.**: Randomised, double-blind, double-dummy phase 3 trial at 56 Chinese hospitals (n=337) of the third-generation EGFR TKI limertinib versus gefitinib as first-line treatment for EGFR exon 19 deletion or L858R advanced NSCLC. Median ICR-assessed PFS was 20.7 vs 9.7 months (HR 0.44, 95% CI 0.34-0.58; p<0.0001), with grade 3+ treatment-related AEs in 25% in both arms. Supports limertinib's first-line approval in China; the comparator is a first-generation TKI rather than osimertinib, the population is Chinese only, and OS is not reported in the abstract.
  Shi Y, Wu L, Ji Y et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40550238/
- **Limertinib: First Approval.**: Drug-approval profile of limertinib (Aoyixin), a third-generation EGFR TKI. It was first approved in China in January 2025 for T790M-positive NSCLC after EGFR-TKI progression, and in April 2025 for first-line treatment of exon 19 deletion or L858R advanced NSCLC. Records a new EGFR-directed approval, so far limited to China; the profile adds no new efficacy data.
  Lee A. *Drugs*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40549097/
- **Targeting WEE1 to Overcome ARID1A Mutation-Driven Osimertinib Resistance in EGFR-Mutant Lung Cancer.**: Combines a mouse model of osimertinib-resistant leptomeningeal carcinomatosis with NGS, functional assays, a CRISPR-Cas9 screen and analysis of clinical samples. ARID1A mutations were the most common resistance alteration; ARID1A-mutant cells were vulnerable to WEE1 inhibition, and combined EGFR plus WEE1 inhibition suppressed tumour growth in the mouse model. In patients on osimertinib, ARID1A mutation was associated with shorter median PFS (6.25 vs 18.0 months) and OS (17.0 vs 34.0 months). Points to ARID1A as a resistance biomarker and WEE1 as a candidate combination target; the therapeutic evidence is preclinical, and the abstract does not give the size of the clinical cohort.
  Fukuda K, Nanjo S, Takeuchi S et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40518016/
- **A Pooled Analysis of Datopotamab Deruxtecan in Patients With EGFR-Mutated NSCLC.**: Exploratory pooled analysis of 117 heavily pretreated patients (median 3 prior lines) with EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC who received datopotamab deruxtecan 6 mg/kg in phase II TROPION-Lung05 or phase III TROPION-Lung01. Confirmed ORR was 43% (95% CI 34-52), median DoR 7.0 months, median PFS 5.8 months and median OS 15.6 months, with grade 3+ treatment-related AEs in 23%. Supports Dato-DXd as a post-osimertinib, post-chemotherapy option; this is a post-hoc pooled single-arm analysis without the docetaxel comparison and is mostly Asian (69%).
  Ahn MJ, Lisberg A, Goto Y et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40516821/
- **PMDA regulatory update on approval and revision of the precautions for use of anticancer drugs; approval of belantamab mafodotin for multiple myeloma, asciminib for leukemia, osimertinib for lung cancer, amivantamab for lung cancer, and pembrolizumab for pleural mesothelioma in Japan.**: Regulatory update from Japan's PMDA on recent anticancer drug approvals, including osimertinib and amivantamab for lung cancer (no abstract available). It records approvals of two EGFR-directed lung cancer drugs in Japan. The available text does not give the specific indications (for example which osimertinib setting or amivantamab regimen) or approval dates.
  Matsumura N, Mandai M. *Int J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40481943/
- **Sacituzumab tirumotecan versus docetaxel for previously treated EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer: multicentre, open label, randomised controlled trial.**: Multicentre, open-label randomised trial at 48 Chinese centres (n=137, 2:1) of the TROP2 ADC sacituzumab tirumotecan versus docetaxel in EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC after EGFR-TKI and platinum chemotherapy. BIRC-assessed ORR was 45% vs 16%, median PFS 6.9 vs 2.8 months (HR 0.30) and 12-month OS 73% vs 54% (HR 0.49; crossover-adjusted HR 0.36), with fewer grade 3+ treatment-related AEs (56% vs 72%). Randomised evidence for a post-TKI ADC option; limitations are the open-label design, a modest-sized Chinese-only population, ORR as primary endpoint and short follow-up (12.2 months).
  Fang W, Li X, Wang Q et al. *BMJ*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40473437/
- **HS-10375, a selective EGFR C797S tyrosine kinase inhibitor, in advanced non-small cell lung cancer.**: Preclinical work and a first-in-human phase 1 trial of HS-10375, a selective EGFR C797S inhibitor (fourth-generation TKI). It was active against double and triple C797S mutants in cell lines and xenografts; in 28 TKI-pretreated patients the MTD was 150 mg daily, with vomiting, appetite loss and AST rise as the most common TRAEs, and tumour shrinkage was reported in one heavily pretreated patient. Early clinical proof of a C797S-targeted TKI, but efficacy in patients is essentially anecdotal (one response) and the trial was a dose-escalation study.
  Zhan J, Xue J, Wu L et al. *J Transl Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40468352/
- **Savolitinib plus osimertinib in epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR)-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer with MET overexpression and/or amplification following disease progression on osimertinib: primary results from the phase II SAVANNAH study.**: Primary analysis of the phase II SAVANNAH study of savolitinib plus osimertinib in EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC with MET overexpression and/or amplification after progression on osimertinib (341 treated with the combination; primary efficacy population n=80 with MET IHC3+/90%+ and/or FISH10+ on 300 mg twice daily). Investigator-assessed ORR was 56.3% (BICR 55.0%), median DoR 7.1 months and median PFS 7.4 months (BICR 7.5); the most common AEs were peripheral oedema (46%), nausea and diarrhoea. Supports an oral MET-directed combination after osimertinib in a high-MET subgroup selected by a revised biomarker cutoff; the efficacy population is non-randomised and the cutoff was raised after a preliminary analysis.
  de Marinis F, Kim TM, Bonanno L et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40461383/
- **Neoadjuvant Osimertinib for Resectable EGFR-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer.**: NeoADAURA: randomised phase III trial (n=358) in resectable stage II-IIIB EGFR-mutated NSCLC comparing neoadjuvant osimertinib plus chemotherapy, osimertinib alone, and placebo plus chemotherapy before surgery. Major pathological response was 26% and 25% vs 2% (both P<.0001); 12-month EFS was 93%, 95% and 83%, but with only 15% data maturity; grade 3+ AEs occurred in 36%, 13% and 33%. Establishes neoadjuvant osimertinib as improving pathological response; the primary endpoint is a surrogate, and EFS and OS benefit are not yet established.
  He J, Tsuboi M, Weder W et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40454705/
- **A trispecific antibody targeting EGFR/cMET/VEGF-A demonstrates multiple mechanisms of action to inhibit wild-type and mutant NSCLC animal models.**: Preclinical study of TAVO412, a trispecific antibody against EGFR, cMET and VEGF-A. It blocked ligand-induced EGFR/cMET phosphorylation, degraded the receptors and had Fc effector function, with stronger antitumour activity than amivantamab and cetuximab in xenografts with varying mutant and wild-type EGFR/cMET. Combined with osimertinib, lazertinib, docetaxel or radiotherapy it produced complete, durable regressions. A possible next step beyond EGFR-MET bispecifics; xenograft data only and no clinical results.
  Jin Y, Sun P, Chen P et al. *Front Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40452841/
- **Zipalertinib in Patients With Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor Exon 20 Insertion-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Previously Treated With Platinum-Based Chemotherapy With or Without Amivantamab.**: REZILIENT1 is an open-label phase I/II trial of zipalertinib 100 mg twice daily in EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC after platinum chemotherapy, with or without amivantamab or other ex20ins-targeted drugs (244 treated; 176 in the primary efficacy population, about 8 months' follow-up). Confirmed ORR by central review was 35.2% (95% CI 28.2-42.8) with median DOR 8.8 months; ORR was 40% after chemotherapy only, 30% after amivantamab only and 14.3% after amivantamab plus another ex20ins agent, and 30.9% in 68 patients with CNS metastases. Grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs were infrequent (anaemia 7%, pneumonitis and rash 2.5% each). Single-arm data with short follow-up and small post-amivantamab subgroups; no randomised comparator.
  Piotrowska Z, Passaro A, Nguyen D et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40450572/
- **A pomalidomide-based gefitinib PROTAC degrader effectively inhibits lung cancer progression in EGFR-TKIs-acquired resistant models by targeting EGFR degradation and ETFA-mediated ATP generation.**: Preclinical study of P-G, a gefitinib-pomalidomide PROTAC that degrades EGFR (via proteasome and autophagy-lysosome pathways) and also binds ETFA, promoting apoptosis in EGFR-mutant and TKI-resistant cells. EGFR degradation lasted more than 72 hours after washout, and P-G significantly suppressed tumour growth in a gefitinib-acquired-resistant HCC827 xenograft with acceptable biosafety. A degrader approach to TKI resistance; tested in a first-generation-TKI resistance model only, with no C797S or osimertinib-resistant models and no clinical data.
  Li L, Li Y, Ma X et al. *Bioorg Chem*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40815992/
- **Dacomitinib in the treatment of EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer with brain metastases: an open-label, multicenter, phase II study.**: Open-label, multicentre phase II study of first-line dacomitinib 45 mg/day in 15 treatment-naive EGFR-mutant NSCLC patients with three or more brain metastases (at least one >1 cm). Median PFS was 16.8 months, ORR 93.3%, intracranial ORR 66.7% and median intracranial PFS 16.6 months; 40% had grade 3 or higher TEAEs. This adds prospective CNS efficacy data for a second-generation TKI in a high-CNS-burden group. Very small single-arm study with wide confidence intervals and no comparison with osimertinib.
  Yu Y, Pan Y, Zhou J et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40811939/
- **[Chinese Medical Association guideline for clinical diagnosis and treatment of lung cancer (2025 edition)].**: National practice guideline (Chinese Medical Association, 2025 edition) updating the 2024 version across screening, diagnosis, molecular testing and treatment. For advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC it adds combination therapies alongside a broader range of monotherapies, and more options after EGFR-TKI resistance; it also covers newly approved drugs for HER2-mutant disease and transformed SCLC. The abstract does not give specific recommendation grades; the guideline reflects drugs approved in China and Chinese practice. (Published in parallel in Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi, PMID 40796286.)
  Oncology Society of Chinese Medical Association. *Zhonghua Zhong Liu Za Zhi*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40796288/
- **[Chinese Medical Association guideline for clinical diagnosis and treatment of lung cancer (2025 edition)].**: Same 2025 Chinese Medical Association lung cancer guideline as PMID 40796288, published in parallel in Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi. Updates cover combination therapy for advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC, more options after EGFR-TKI resistance, and new drugs for HER2-mutant disease and transformed SCLC. The abstract gives no detailed recommendations, and the guideline is specific to Chinese approvals and practice.
  Oncology Society of Chinese Medical Association. *Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40796286/
- **Longitudinal Circulating Tumor DNA-Guided Resistance Analysis During Second-Line Osimertinib Treatment.**: Prospective multicentre study of 21 evaluable patients on second- or third-line osimertinib with plasma ctDNA (AVENIO panel) every 8 weeks; crizotinib was to be added if MET amplification appeared before progression. In 33% a rise in EGFR VAF preceded radiologic PD (median 9 weeks), and in 33% at least one resistance mechanism was detected before PD (median 14 weeks); no MET amplification was seen, so the intervention arm was never triggered. Post-progression biopsies confirmed plasma findings in 5 of 16 and found additional mechanisms in 5. Small cohort, second-line osimertinib setting (not current first-line practice) and no test of ctDNA-guided treatment switching.
  van der Wel JWT, Jebbink M, van der Noort V et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40792215/
- **Eradicating Drug-tolerant Persister Cells in EGFR-Mutated Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer by Targeting TROP2 with CAR-T Cellular Therapy.**: Using neoadjuvant EGFR-TKI patient samples, cell lines and PDX models, the authors found TROP2 enriched on EGFR-TKI drug-tolerant persister cells. At osimertinib-induced minimal residual disease in vivo, sacituzumab govitecan only modestly delayed recurrence, whereas a single infusion of TROP2-directed CAR-T cells significantly prolonged relapse-free survival, with some cures. This suggests a way to target the residual disease from which resistance emerges. Preclinical mouse data only; CAR-T safety against normal TROP2-expressing tissue has not been tested in patients.
  Baldacci S, Brea EJ, Facchinetti F et al. *Cancer Discov*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40762432/
- **Comprehensive first-in-human phase I/II study of FHND-9041 in patients with EGFR-mutated advanced non-small cell lung cancer.**: Single-arm, multicentre phase I/II study: 87 patients with pretreated T790M-positive NSCLC in dose escalation and expansion (40-180 mg/day), then 37 treatment-naive patients at the recommended phase 2 dose of 80 mg. No DLTs were seen; first-line ORR was 62.2% (23/37) and median PFS 15.5 months, and grade 3/4 related AEs included leukopenia (11.3%) and liver impairment (4.8%). This adds another third-generation TKI to the field, but the first-line PFS appears no better than established agents. Non-randomised, small first-line cohort and no CNS-specific data in the abstract.
  Zeng L, Tong F, Xu X et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40752090/
- **Final Analysis Results and Patient-Reported Outcomes From DESTINY-Lung02-A Dose-Blinded, Randomized, Phase 2 Study of Trastuzumab Deruxtecan in Patients With HER2-Mutant Metastatic NSCLC.**: Randomised (2:1), dose-blinded phase 2 trial of trastuzumab deruxtecan 5.4 vs 6.4 mg/kg in previously treated HER2-mutant metastatic NSCLC (n=102 and 50), final analysis after about 16 months' median follow-up. Confirmed ORR was 50.0% and 56.0%; drug-related grade 3 or higher AEs were 39.6% vs 60.0%, and adjudicated drug-related ILD was 14.9% vs 32.0% (one grade 5 in each arm). Health-related quality of life was maintained, supporting 5.4 mg/kg as the dose. Phase 2 without a non-T-DXd comparator; HER2-mutant rather than EGFR-mutant population.
  Jänne PA, Goto Y, Kubo T et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40749900/
- **A Cyclized Bivalent Aptamer-Based Protein Degrader Targeting Receptor Tyrosine Kinases Overcomes Resistance to Inhibitors in Lung Cancer.**: Preclinical study of AHPC-cb-Ap, a cyclized bivalent aptamer-based PROTAC that degrades c-Met and interacting receptor tyrosine kinases, including EGFR. It restored erlotinib sensitivity in resistant NCI-H1975 (EGFR T790M/L858R) cells; in xenografts pretreated with the degrader, erlotinib reached about 70% antitumour efficacy, comparable to osimertinib. It targets two main resistance routes (on-target EGFR and MET bypass) at once. Single cell-line model, compared with first-generation TKI resistance rather than osimertinib resistance; no clinical data.
  Wu S, Zhou X, Ai L et al. *ACS Nano*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40728393/
- **Discovery of a Novel EGFR PROTAC Degrader against C797S Resistance Mutation with Potent Antitumor Efficacy in NSCLC Treatment.**: Medicinal chemistry study of a series of EGFR-C797S PROTAC degraders. Lead 9ea degraded EGFR-C797S and other main mutants (DC50 2.9 nM, Dmax 93%) through VHL and the proteasome, selectively over wild-type EGFR, and caused cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. In EGFR-C797S xenografts it gave 74.7% tumour growth inhibition at 50 mg/kg and depleted EGFR in tumour tissue. A degrader approach for the main osimertinib on-target resistance mutation; preclinical only, with no pharmacokinetic or toxicology data in the abstract.
  Wang X, Wang C, Ma Y et al. *J Med Chem*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40640988/
- **Efficacy and safety of sunvozertinib monotherapy as first-line treatment in NSCLC patients with EGFR exon 20 insertion mutations: A phase 2, single-center trial.**: Single-centre phase 2 WU-KONG15 (group 4) of sunvozertinib 200 mg daily in 26 treatment-naive EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC patients (99 in the safety set). Median PFS was 10.1 months, confirmed ORR 73.1%, median DOR 10.5 months and estimated median OS 23.1 months; grade 3 or higher TRAEs occurred in 35.4% (CPK increase 10.1%, diarrhoea 8.1%). Clearance of plasma ex20ins ctDNA correlated with response. This is a chemotherapy-free first-line option, but the cohort is small, single-centre and single-arm, and the PFS should be read against amivantamab plus chemotherapy (PAPILLON).
  Xu Y, Chen M, Gao X et al. *Cancer Lett*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40618881/
- **Lazertinib Versus Osimertinib in Previously Untreated EGFR-Mutant Advanced NSCLC: A Randomized, Double-Blind, Exploratory Analysis From MARIPOSA.**: Exploratory comparison of the lazertinib monotherapy arm (n=216) with the osimertinib arm (n=429) in the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial of treatment-naive EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC, the first randomised, double-blind comparison of two third-generation EGFR TKIs. At 22.0 months' median follow-up, median PFS was 18.5 vs 16.6 months (HR 0.98, 95% CI 0.79-1.22), ORR 83% vs 85%, and interim OS HR 1.00; lazertinib caused less QT prolongation. This shows the lazertinib component is roughly equivalent to osimertinib. Exploratory, not powered for equivalence, and the OS data are immature.
  Lee SH, Lu S, Hayashi H et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40617394/
- **Poziotinib for EGFR exon 20-insertion NSCLC: Clinical efficacy of the phase 2 ZENITH trial and differential impact of EGFR exon 20 insertion location on sensitivity.**: Phase 2 ZENITH20 cohort 1 tested poziotinib in 115 previously treated patients with EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC, combined with in vitro and molecular dynamics work. ORR was 14.8% (95% CI 8.9-22.6), so the primary endpoint was not met. In exploratory analyses near-loop insertions did better than far-loop ones (mean tumour shrinkage -25.9% vs -9.8%; PFS 11.1 vs 3.5 months), whereas in EXCLAIM mobocertinib showed no difference by location. Insertion location may therefore matter when choosing an ex20ins TKI; the subgroup findings are exploratory, and the comparison with mobocertinib is across trials.
  Le X, Robichaux JP, Nilsson M et al. *Nat Commun*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40993146/
- **Biological impact of chemotherapy during treatment with EGFR tyrosine kinase inhibitors for non-small cell lung cancer positive for EGFR activating mutations.**: Prospective plasma and tissue biomarker study in 200 patients from the randomised phase III JCOG1404/WJOG8214L trial of EGFR-TKI with or without inserted chemotherapy. In patients with detectable EGFR mutation in baseline cfDNA, inserted chemotherapy improved PFS vs TKI alone (median 17.5 vs 11.8 months); the benefit was also larger with concurrent TP53 mutations (18.8 vs 13.5 months). Chemotherapy reduced EGFR activating and T790M alleles in cfDNA at progression. This supports ctDNA as a way to select patients for TKI-chemotherapy combinations; the subgroup analyses are post hoc, the TKIs were mostly earlier-generation and no interaction tests are given in the abstract.
  Iwama E, Sakai K, Harada T et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40974872/
- **A phase II study of afatinib in combination with pemetrexed and carboplatin in patients with EGFR mutation-positive non-squamous, advanced non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) refractory to first-line osimertinib treatment: NEJ025B study.**: Single-arm phase II study of afatinib 20 mg plus carboplatin-pemetrexed, then afatinib plus pemetrexed maintenance, in 35 evaluable patients with EGFR ex19del/L858R NSCLC after first-line osimertinib failure. The 6-month PFS rate was 57.1% (95% CI 39.3-71.5%), above the 35% threshold; ORR was 51.4%, median PFS 8.2 months and median OS 22.5 months. Diarrhoea (52.8%) and anorexia were common, interstitial pneumonia occurred in 8.3% (one death), and there was one sepsis death. A possible post-osimertinib option, but single-arm, small and with no comparison against chemotherapy alone.
  Nagashima H, Fukuhara T, Utsumi Y et al. *Eur J Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40957291/
- **[Chinese expert consensus on epidermal growth factor receptor tyrosine kinase inhibitors for the treatment of non-small cell lung cancer (2025 edition)].**: Expert consensus from Chinese oncology associations, updating the 2023 Chinese guideline on EGFR-TKIs in stage IV NSCLC, to guide the choice among the many EGFR TKIs approved in China. The recommendations were updated using recent clinical evidence. The abstract gives no specific recommendations or levels of evidence; it is a consensus rather than a systematic guideline, and it reflects Chinese approvals.
  Medical Oncology Branch of China International Exchange and Promotive Association for Medical and Health Care, Chinese Association for Clinical Oncologists. *Zhonghua Yi Xue Za Zhi*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40953975/
- **Overall Survival with Amivantamab-Lazertinib in EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC**: Protocol-specified final OS analysis of the phase 3 MARIPOSA trial, which randomised previously untreated EGFR exon 19 deletion/L858R advanced NSCLC to amivantamab-lazertinib, osimertinib or lazertinib (429 per arm in the two compared groups). Over a median follow-up of 37.8 months, amivantamab-lazertinib prolonged OS versus osimertinib (HR 0.75, 95% CI 0.61-0.92, P = 0.005; 3-year OS 60% vs 51%). Grade 3 or higher adverse events were much more frequent with the combination (80% vs 52%), particularly skin events, venous thromboembolism and infusion reactions, so the survival gain comes with substantial added toxicity. Open-label design and the toxicity burden are the main limitations for routine first-line use. Yang JC, Lu S, Hayashi H et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40923797/
- **Survival with Osimertinib plus Chemotherapy in EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC**: Final OS analysis of the phase 3 FLAURA2 trial in 557 treatment-naive patients with EGFR-mutated (exon 19 deletion or L858R) advanced NSCLC randomised to osimertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed or osimertinib alone. Median OS was 47.5 versus 37.6 months (HR 0.77, 95% CI 0.61-0.96, P = 0.02). Grade 3 or higher adverse events occurred in 70% versus 34%, with osimertinib discontinuation in 12% versus 7%; the authors describe the excess events as largely reversible. The trial was open-label and does not compare against amivantamab-lazertinib, so relative positioning of the two intensified first-line regimens remains untested. Jänne PA, Planchard D, Kobayashi K et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41104938/
- **Sacituzumab Tirumotecan in EGFR-TKI-Resistant, EGFR-Mutated Advanced NSCLC**: Phase 3 OptiTROP-Lung04 randomised patients with EGFR-mutated nonsquamous advanced NSCLC progressing after EGFR-TKI therapy 1:1 to the TROP2 ADC sacituzumab tirumotecan or pemetrexed plus platinum. Median PFS was 8.3 versus 4.3 months (HR 0.49, 95% CI 0.39-0.62) and OS was longer with the ADC (HR 0.60, 95% CI 0.44-0.82, P = 0.001; 18-month OS 65.8% vs 48.0%). Grade 3 or higher treatment-related events were similar (58.0% vs 53.8%, mainly neutropenia), with fewer treatment-related serious events on the ADC. This is the first phase 3 OS benefit for an ADC in this post-TKI setting; the trial population was predominantly Chinese, which may limit generalisability. Fang W, Wu L, Meng X et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41124220/
- **Sevabertinib in Advanced HER2-Mutant Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer**: Open-label phase 1-2 SOHO-01 study of the oral reversible HER2 TKI sevabertinib 20 mg twice daily in locally advanced or metastatic HER2-mutant NSCLC, in three cohorts by prior therapy. Objective response was 64% (95% CI 53-75; median PFS 8.3 months) in previously treated HER2-therapy-naive patients, 38% after prior HER2 ADC, and 71% in the treatment-naive cohort. Diarrhoea was near-universal (84-91%, grade 3 or higher in 5-23%) and 31% had grade 3 or higher drug-related events. Single-arm cohorts and immature PFS data in the first-line cohort limit conclusions about durability. Le X, Kim TM, Loong HH et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41104928/
- **Phase II Dose-Randomized Study of Sunvozertinib in Platinum-Pretreated NSCLC With EGFR Exon 20 Insertion Mutations (WU-KONG1B)**: Multinational phase 2 study randomising platinum-pretreated EGFR exon20ins NSCLC to sunvozertinib 200 mg or 300 mg once daily, with additional non-randomised 300 mg patients. Confirmed ORR by blinded independent review was 45.9% (200 mg-rand, n = 85), 47.2% (300 mg-rand, n = 89) and 45.8% (300 mg-all, n = 107). The 300 mg dose gave higher response in patients with baseline brain metastases (52.4% vs 28.6%) and after prior amivantamab (41.7% vs 25%) and longer DoR (13.8 vs 11.1 months), but more grade 3 or higher diarrhoea (18% vs 2.2%). Single-arm efficacy comparison across non-randomised patients and short follow-up temper the dose-selection message. Yang JC, Wang M, Doucet L et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40923280/
- **Adjuvant icotinib for resected EGFR-mutated stage II-IIIA NSCLC (ICTAN, GASTO1002)**: Phase 3 trial randomising 251 patients with completely resected EGFR-mutated stage II-IIIA NSCLC after adjuvant chemotherapy to icotinib for 12 months, icotinib for 6 months, or observation. Both icotinib durations improved DFS (HR 0.40 and 0.41 vs observation, both P < 0.001) and OS (HR 0.55, P = 0.032; HR 0.56, P = 0.038), with no difference between 12 and 6 months (DFS HR 0.97). Grade 3 or higher adverse events were 8.3%, 6.0% and 2.4%. The trial was terminated early and is underpowered for the duration comparison, so the suggestion that 6 months suffices needs confirmation. Li N, Ou W, Cheng C et al. *Signal Transduct Target Ther*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40866342/
- **COMPEL: osimertinib plus platinum-based chemotherapy after progression on first-line osimertinib**: Global, randomised, double-blind phase 3 study (98 patients, 49 per arm) of continuing osimertinib with platinum-pemetrexed versus placebo plus chemotherapy after non-CNS progression on first-line osimertinib. Median PFS was 8.4 versus 4.4 months (HR 0.43, 95% CI 0.27-0.70) and median OS 15.9 versus 9.8 months (HR 0.71, 95% CI 0.42-1.23), with CNS PFS also favouring the combination in patients without baseline CNS disease. Grade 3 or higher adverse events were 63% versus 46%. The sample size is small and the OS confidence interval crosses 1, so the OS signal is suggestive rather than definitive. Peled N, Tufman A, Sequist LV et al. *ESMO Open*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41139117/
- **Amivantamab-Chemotherapy in NSCLC with EGFR Exon 20 Insertions: crossover-adjusted endpoints from PAPILLON**: Secondary analyses of the phase 3 PAPILLON trial (308 participants) of first-line amivantamab plus carboplatin-pemetrexed versus chemotherapy in EGFR exon20ins NSCLC. Median time to treatment discontinuation was 13.2 versus 7.5 months (HR 0.38) and time to subsequent therapy 17.7 versus 9.9 months (HR 0.35). With 65 of 155 control patients crossing over to amivantamab, crossover-adjusted OS hazard ratios were 0.52-0.60 across three methods, consistent with an OS benefit obscured in the unadjusted interim analysis. Crossover adjustment relies on modelling assumptions, and the interim OS data remain immature. Sanborn RE, Zhou C, Tang KJ et al. *Target Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41184595/
- **Osimertinib Plus Savolitinib After First-Line Osimertinib With MET Amplification (ORCHARD)**: Module of the phase 2 ORCHARD biomarker-directed platform study enrolling 32 patients with EGFR-mutated NSCLC and MET amplification (at least 4 copies over tumour ploidy) on post-progression biopsy after first-line osimertinib. Confirmed ORR was 47% (80% CI 34-60), median DoR 14.5 months, median PFS 7.6 months and median OS 20.7 months; response trended higher with MET copy number of 10 or more. Grade 3 or higher treatment-emergent events occurred in 44%, most often pneumonia. Single-arm design with a small, biomarker-selected cohort means efficacy cannot be separated from prognosis of the selected group. Le X, Baik C, Cho BC et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41130408/
- **Patritumab deruxtecan (HER3-DXd) in active brain metastases of NSCLC (TUXEDO-3)**: Single-arm phase 2 cohort (20 patients) of HER3-DXd in advanced NSCLC with newly diagnosed or locally progressing brain metastases. The primary endpoint was met with an intracranial ORR of 30.0% (95% CI 11.9-54.3); all six responders had non-squamous disease and only one carried a driver mutation (KRAS G12C). Treatment-related adverse events occurred in 80%, with grade 3-4 neutropenia in 15% and febrile neutropenia in 10%. Only five patients had activating driver mutations and median follow-up was 5.3 months, so the data say little about CNS activity specifically in EGFR-mutant disease. Fuereder T, Garde-Noguera J, García-Mosquera JJ et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41167214/
- **Phase 2 Study of Amivantamab Plus Lazertinib in Previously Treated EGFR-Mutant Lung Cancers With Brain and Leptomeningeal Metastases**: Investigator-initiated two-cohort phase 2 trial in 20 patients with brain metastases (BrM) and 21 with cytologically confirmed leptomeningeal disease (LMD) after a median of 2 prior therapies. Composite ORR was 50% (95% CI 27-73) in BrM and 33% (95% CI 15-57) in LMD, with median PFS 5.8 and 7.8 months and median OS 17.4 and 14.4 months. Small single-arm cohorts with mixed EGFR genotypes (del19, L858R, exon20ins, uncommon) limit precision, but it is among the first prospective datasets including LMD patients. Chen MF, Lee JJ, Choudhury NJ et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41139066/
- **Lazertinib plus pemetrexed in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with leptomeningeal metastases (KCSG LU 21-01, LAZARUS)**: Prospective single-arm phase 2 trial in 36 Korean patients with cytologically confirmed leptomeningeal carcinomatosis treated with lazertinib 240 mg daily plus pemetrexed. Overall response was 24.1% with disease control 96.6%, median PFS 9.3 months and median OS 9.9 months; CSF cytology conversion was low (9.4% and 11.5% at cycles 2 and 3), while quality of life and neurological symptoms improved. CSF/plasma concentration ratio correlated with OS at cycle 3. Single-arm design, small size and low cytological conversion mean the survival figures should be read as benchmarks rather than proof of benefit. Jung HA, Kim TM, Kim HR et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41202701/
- **Central Nervous System Outcomes of Lazertinib: Pooled Analysis From LASER201 and LASER301**: Pooled post hoc analysis of 64 treatment-naive patients with EGFR-mutated advanced NSCLC and stable CNS metastases treated with lazertinib, of whom 24 had at least one measurable CNS lesion. Median intracranial PFS was 27.7 months (95% CI 15.7-32.8); among measurable lesions intracranial ORR was 92% and DCR 96%, with median intracranial DoR 26.5 months. Grade 3 or higher treatment-emergent events occurred in 55%, commonly paraesthesia and rash. This is a non-randomised pooled subgroup with few measurable CNS lesions, so the high response rates are imprecise. Yang JC, Ahn MJ, Kim JH et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41067998/
- **Osimertinib plus consolidative radiotherapy for advanced EGFR-mutant NSCLC**: Multicentre single-arm phase 2 trial at two US sites in which 42 TKI-naive patients with advanced EGFR exon 19/21-mutant NSCLC started osimertinib, with consolidative (mostly stereotactic) radiotherapy to residual disease at best response in 32 (76%). At a median follow-up of 35.7 months, median PFS was 32.3 months (95% CI 21.9-51.7), median OS 45 months (95% CI 39.3-56.4) and median osimertinib duration 32.4 months; toxicities were mostly grade 1-2, with pneumonitis in 2 patients (one grade 4). Single-arm design with no osimertinib-alone comparator, and selection of patients fit enough to receive consolidation, preclude attributing the long PFS to radiotherapy. Sampath S, Rashdan S, Iyengar P et al. *EClinicalMedicine*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41054436/
- **Enhanced Versus Standard Dermatologic Management With Amivantamab-Lazertinib: The COCOON Trial**: Randomised phase 2 trial in 201 patients starting first-line amivantamab plus lazertinib, comparing a prophylactic regimen (oral doxycycline/minocycline weeks 1-12, clindamycin 1% scalp lotion, chlorhexidine 4% for nails, ceramide moisturiser) with local standard of care. Grade 2 or higher dermatologic adverse events of interest by week 12 fell from 75% to 42% (OR 0.24, 95% CI 0.13-0.45, P < 0.0001), with the largest gains for face/body (26% vs 60%) and scalp (10% vs 26%) and better patient-reported outcomes. Open-label design and 7.1-month median follow-up limit assessment of longer-term skin toxicity and of any effect on treatment persistence. Cho BC, Li W, Spira AI et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40923969/
- **Gefitinib with or without bevacizumab in EGFR L858R-positive advanced non-squamous NSCLC (BEVA-FLFX-001)**: Open-label randomised phase 2 trial in 81 patients with stage III/IV L858R-mutant NSCLC. Adding bevacizumab to gefitinib improved median PFS (15.1 vs 8.2 months, P < 0.01) and ORR (90.2% vs 59.0%, P < 0.01), with hypertension confined to the combination arm. Plasma ctDNA positivity at 6 weeks predicted shorter PFS, and a greater than 98% fall in maximal variant allele frequency identified patients with longer PFS in the combination arm (17.0 vs 5.4 months). The comparator is a first-generation TKI rather than current third-generation standard, the sample is small, and OS was not reported. Zhao X, Wu X, Wang H et al. *Transl Lung Cancer Res*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41234598/
- **Enozertinib Is a Selective, Brain-Penetrant EGFR Inhibitor for EGFR Exon 20 and Atypical Mutations**: Preclinical discovery and development package for enozertinib (formerly ORIC-114), an oral irreversible EGFR inhibitor with high kinome selectivity, showing potency and tumour regressions across atypical EGFR-mutant models plus evidence of brain penetration. Initial phase 1 experience in advanced NSCLC with atypical EGFR mutations included one patient with an exon 20 insertion who had a sustained complete response in systemic and brain lesions. Clinical evidence is a single reported case within an ongoing dose-finding trial, so efficacy and safety in patients are not yet established. Junttila MR, Repellin CE, Salaniwal S et al. *Cancer Res*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41196054/
- **AZ14289671, a selective blood-brain barrier penetrant irreversible TKI targeting EGFR exon 20 insertions**: Preclinical characterisation of an oral, irreversible, exon20ins-selective EGFR TKI that spares wild-type EGFR. In cell lines and both cell-line-derived and patient-derived xenograft models harbouring various exon 20 insertions, the compound produced strong pathway inhibition and sustained tumour regressions with minimal wild-type activity, and crossed the blood-brain barrier. No clinical data are presented, and the abstract gives no quantitative regression or CNS exposure figures, so the translational step remains to be shown. Swaih AM, Talbot S, Savoca A et al. *Cell Rep Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40858103/
- **EGFR-targeted liposomal PROTAC with epigenetic regulation for osimertinib-resistant lung cancer**: Preclinical study of a split-and-mix liposomal PROTAC assembled from DSPE-PEG2000-E3 ligand and DSPE-PEG2000-EGFR ligand, co-loaded with the class I HDAC inhibitor MS-275 (GM-protac), designed to degrade mutant EGFR through both E3-dependent and lysosome-autophagy routes while also hitting bypass pathways. The construct was selectively toxic to gefitinib- and osimertinib-resistant cells and inhibited tumour growth with little toxicity in both resistance models, with effects on BIM-dependent apoptosis, c-Met, PD-L1, HER2 and NF-kB/PI3K-AKT signalling. Mouse models only, no pharmacokinetic or formulation-scalability data, so clinical relevance is speculative. Wang D, Liu Y, Chen Y et al. *Adv Sci (Weinh)*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40842076/
- **Targeted degradation of EGFR 19Del by PROTACs suppresses tumor growth in NSCLC**: Rationally designed gefitinib-CRBN PROTAC series; compound 14 (814 Da) degraded EGFR del19 and L858R with DC50 of 0.26 nM and 20.57 nM respectively, spared wild-type EGFR, suppressed downstream signalling and killed HCC827 cells (96-h IC50 4.91 nM) without affecting H1299, HeLa or H1975 viability up to 1 microM. Degradation was ubiquitin-proteasome dependent, and intraperitoneal compound 14 (30 mg/kg) inhibited HCC827 xenograft growth with reduced tumour EGFR levels. Activity is limited to classical sensitising mutations (no effect in H1975), and oral bioavailability is not demonstrated. Piao L, Gao Y, Su Y et al. *Front Pharmacol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41208859/
- **FDA approves second-line TROP2-directed antibody-drug conjugate Dato-DXd for EGFR-mutated NSCLC**: Short news report of the US regulatory approval of datopotamab deruxtecan for previously treated EGFR-mutated NSCLC, relevant because it changes the label and the available post-TKI options. No trial numbers, endpoints or safety data are given in the item itself, so the supporting evidence must be read from the primary trial publications. Lawrence L. *Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41178651/
- **EGFR exon 20-targeting sunvozertinib granted accelerated approval for advanced NSCLC**: Short news report of the FDA accelerated approval of sunvozertinib for advanced NSCLC with EGFR exon 20 insertion mutations, a label change that adds an oral option in this subgroup. The item contains no efficacy or safety figures, and accelerated approval means confirmatory trial data are still pending. Lawrence L. *Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41178650/
- **Trastuzumab Deruxtecan in Patients With HER2-Overexpressing NSCLC: Results From Part 1 of the Open-Label, Multicenter, Phase 1b DESTINY-Lung03 Trial.**: Open-label multi-arm phase 1b study of T-DXd plus durvalumab and cisplatin (n=11) or carboplatin (n=24), or T-DXd 5.4 mg/kg monotherapy (n=36), in pretreated HER2-overexpressing (IHC 3+/2+) metastatic NSCLC. Confirmed ORR was 44.4% with monotherapy and 37.5% with the carboplatin combination. Drug-related serious AEs occurred in 63.6%, 37.5% and 16.7% of the three arms, with grade 5 DLTs in the cisplatin arm. The authors say the data support T-DXd monotherapy but not the platinum/durvalumab combination in this setting. The arms were small and non-randomised, and the population was not EGFR-selected.
  Planchard D, Kim HR, Suksombooncharoen T et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41448488/
- **First-line serplulimab plus chemotherapy with or without HLX04 versus chemotherapy in locally advanced or metastatic non-squamous non-small-cell lung cancer (ASTRUM-002): a randomised, double-blind, multicentre phase 3 trial.**: Three-arm double-blind phase 3 trial (n=636 randomised, 72 Chinese hospitals) in non-squamous NSCLC without EGFR sensitising mutations or ALK/ROS1 rearrangements. Median PFS was 12.6 months with serplulimab+bevacizumab biosimilar+chemotherapy, 11.0 months with serplulimab+chemotherapy and 5.6 months with chemotherapy (B vs C HR 0.55). Adding the bevacizumab biosimilar gave no significant PFS gain (A vs B HR 0.86, p=0.25). The trial was restricted to Chinese patients (94% Han) and excluded EGFR-mutant disease, so it bears on the driver-negative comparator rather than on EGFR-mutant NSCLC.
  Wang L, Hao X, Hao Y et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41354044/
- **Efficacy and safety with aumolertinib plus anlotinib for untreated EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases.**: Single-arm phase 2 trial GASTO-1063 (n=87) of first-line aumolertinib plus anlotinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases. Intracranial ORR was 71.3%, median intracranial PFS 30.2 months and median overall PFS 22.5 months; median OS was not reached. Outcomes were worse with L858R and TP53 co-mutations. These are CNS efficacy data for a TKI-antiangiogenic combination, but there was no comparator arm against TKI alone.
  Chen J, Pan Y, Li M et al. *NPJ Precis Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41326640/
- **A prospective, multicenter, comprehensive genomic profile signature study in patients with EGFR-mutant advanced non-small cell lung cancer at the first-line treatment failure of osimertinib.**: Prospective multicentre study (NCT05219162) with paired tissue and plasma NGS in 149 patients progressing on first-line osimertinib. In tissue, MET amplification was found in 30.9%, EGFR amplification in 32.9%, C797S in 3.4% and TP53 mutation in 69.8%. Plasma profiling showed 90.7-100% specificity against tissue, and plasma ddPCR agreed with FISH for MET amplification in 94.4% of cases. This is a large prospective map of post-osimertinib resistance that bears on MET-directed treatment selection. It is descriptive, without treatment outcomes, and the MET concordance analysis used only 36 samples.
  Shi Y, Lv D, Feng W et al. *Signal Transduct Target Ther*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41326340/
- **Amivantamab Plus Lazertinib in Atypical EGFR-Mutated Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Results From CHRYSALIS-2.**: CHRYSALIS-2 cohort C (n=105) tested amivantamab plus lazertinib in atypical EGFR mutations (G719X, L861X, S768I), in patients who were treatment-naive or had prior 1G/2G TKI. ORR was 52%, median DoR 14.1 months and median PFS 11.1 months. In treatment-naive patients, ORR was 57% and median PFS 19.5 months. This supports a chemotherapy-free option for uncommon mutations. It was a single-arm cohort with an investigator-assessed primary endpoint, and the structural-subgroup numbers were small.
  Tomasini P, Wang Y, Li Y et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41325571/
- **Savolitinib Plus Osimertinib in Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-Mutated, MET-Amplified Advanced Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: A Randomized Phase II trial.**: Randomised phase 2 trial (n=30) of savolitinib plus osimertinib vs savolitinib plus placebo in EGFR-mutant, MET-amplified NSCLC after osimertinib. ORR was 57% vs 13% and median PFS 7.4 vs 1.6 months. With higher MET cutoffs, ORR was 63% vs 29%. This supports keeping osimertinib alongside a MET inhibitor when MET-driven resistance develops. The trial was very small and terminated early, and the question is being re-addressed in SAVANNAH.
  Yang JC, Chen YM, Batra U et al. *Clin Lung Cancer*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41308232/
- **RELAY+: Final Overall Survival With Ramucirumab Plus Gefitinib in Patients With Untreated EGFR-Mutated Metastatic NSCLC.**: Final OS of the single-arm exploratory RELAY+ study of ramucirumab plus gefitinib in East Asian patients with untreated EGFR-mutant NSCLC without CNS metastases, with ramucirumab plus osimertinib after T790M-positive progression. At a median follow-up of 42.5 months, median OS was 47.4 months: 51.9 months for L858R and 38.4 months for ex19del. Grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs occurred in 62.2%. This is an updated survival dataset for a VEGF-combination strategy. It is single-arm, excluded CNS metastases, used a 1G TKI backbone and had a modest sample size (about 82 patients).
  Nishio M, Seto T, Reck M et al. *JTO Clin Res Rep*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41278399/
- **First-line treatment in EGFR-mutated non-small cell lung cancer: brief report of an individual patient data comparison of phase 3 clinical trials.**: Indirect comparison of FLAURA2 (osimertinib+chemotherapy) and MARIPOSA (amivantamab+lazertinib) using reconstructed individual patient data. PFS modestly favoured osimertinib+chemotherapy in the ITT population (HR 0.79), with no OS difference. Osimertinib+chemotherapy did better in patients with CNS metastases (PFS HR 0.63; intracranial PFS HR 0.52) and with L858R. The authors call it hypothesis-generating, since it is an indirect, non-randomised comparison and subgroup OS could not be compared.
  Torchia A, Sabatini A, Bengala E et al. *Lung Cancer*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41270639/
- **Clinical Significance of MTAP Deletions and Their Overlap With Concurrent Oncogenic Driver Alterations Including EGFR in NSCLC.**: MSK-IMPACT analysis of 4926 NSCLCs found MTAP deletions in 10%. Among stage IV MTAP-deleted tumours, 48% also had EGFR mutations. Baseline MTAP deletion was linked to shorter time to osimertinib discontinuation (19.0 vs 24.9 months), with no significant OS difference. MTAP deletion was acquired at osimertinib resistance in 9 of 69 paired cases (13%), and one EGFR-mutant, MTAP-deleted patient had a partial response to a PRMT5 inhibitor. This suggests a targetable resistance subgroup. The data are retrospective from a single institution, and the clinical evidence for the drug is one patient.
  Ross JS, Thummalapalli R, Febres-Aldana CA et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41260457/
- **Andamertinib in Advanced NSCLC With EGFR Exon 20 Insertions After Platinum-Based Chemotherapy or Immunotherapy: Results From the Phase 2 KANNON Study.**: Single-arm phase 2 KANNON study (n=92) of andamertinib 240 mg in pretreated EGFR exon 20 insertion NSCLC covering about 30 insertion subtypes. Confirmed ORR by independent review was 42.7%, median PFS 6.2 months and 12-month OS 70.5%. Systemic ORR in the 38 patients with brain metastases was 47.4%. Grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs occurred in 40.2%, with no ILD reported. This adds another oral exon 20 option, but there was no comparator, follow-up was short (15.4 months) and the patients were all Chinese.
  Yang JJ, Mu Y, Wang ZH et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41248848/
New trial registrations and posted results (ClinicalTrials.gov):

#### Trial registrations and posted results

- **NCT06908772: Glumetinib plus osimertinib, first line**: New phase 2/3 registration by Shanghai JMT-Bio (n=390, not yet recruiting). Arms are the MET TKI glumetinib plus osimertinib vs osimertinib plus glumetinib placebo as first-line treatment of locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC. The osimertinib backbone implies an EGFR-mutant population, but the summary does not state the biomarker selection. Registered 2025-04-03. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06908772
- **NCT06927986: SYS6010 vs platinum chemotherapy in EGFR-mutant NSCLC**: New phase 3 registration by CSPC Megalith (n=380, recruiting). The EGFR-directed ADC SYS6010 is compared with pemetrexed plus cisplatin/carboplatin in EGFR-mutated locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC. Registered 2025-04-15. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06927986
- **NCT07128199: Adjuvant zipalertinib in resected NSCLC with uncommon EGFR mutations**: New phase 3 registration by Taiho Oncology (n=360, active, not recruiting). Arms are zipalertinib (TAS6417) vs matching placebo after resection of early-stage NSCLC with uncommon EGFR mutations. Registered 2025-08-17. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07128199
- **NCT06970639: Furmonertinib plus chemotherapy vs osimertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with brain metastases**: New international phase 3 registration by Allist (n=380, recruiting). Arms are furmonertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed (A) vs osimertinib monotherapy (B), with an exploratory furmonertinib monotherapy arm (C) and a 30-patient safety run-in. Registered 2025-05-14. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06970639
- **NCT07005102: Telisotuzumab adizutecan plus osimertinib in EGFR-mutant NSCLC**: New phase 2/3 registration by AbbVie (n=854, recruiting). Dose escalation of telisotuzumab adizutecan (a c-Met ADC) with osimertinib is followed by randomisation against standard of care (osimertinib plus platinum-pemetrexed). Registered 2025-06-05. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07005102
- **NCT07182682: Adjuvant sunvozertinib for EGFR exon 20 insertion or PACC mutations**: New phase 3 registration by Dizal (Jiangsu) (n=360, recruiting). Sunvozertinib is compared with placebo after radical surgery for stage IB-IIIA NSCLC with EGFR exon20ins or PACC mutations, with or without adjuvant chemotherapy. Registered 2025-09-19. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07182682
- **NCT06829459: Glumetinib plus osimertinib vs chemotherapy after EGFR-TKI resistance with MET alteration**: New open-label phase 3 registration by Shanghai JMT-Bio (n=350, recruiting). Patients with MET amplification and/or overexpression after EGFR-TKIs are randomised 1:1 to glumetinib plus osimertinib vs platinum-pemetrexed. Registered 2025-02-17. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06829459
- **NCT07185997: Firmonertinib vs osimertinib/afatinib in EGFR PACC mutations**: New global phase 3 registration by ArriVent (n=480, recruiting). First-line firmonertinib 240 mg QD is compared with the investigator's choice of osimertinib 80 mg or afatinib 40 mg, randomised 1:1, in advanced NSCLC with EGFR PACC mutations. Registered 2025-09-22. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07185997
- **NCT06970782: Vebreltinib plus PLB1004 vs chemotherapy after EGFR-TKI failure with MET alteration**: New phase 3 registration by Avistone (n=278, not yet recruiting). The MET TKI vebreltinib plus the EGFR TKI PLB1004 is compared with platinum-pemetrexed in EGFR-mutant NSCLC with MET amplification/overexpression after EGFR-TKI failure. Registered 2025-05-14. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06970782
- **NCT07109531: ASKC202 plus limertinib vs chemotherapy after EGFR-TKI progression with MET alteration**: New phase 3 registration by Jiangsu Aosaikang (n=286, recruiting). Arms are ASKC202 plus limertinib vs platinum-pemetrexed in NSCLC with MET amplification/overexpression after EGFR-TKI progression. Registered 2025-08-07. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07109531
- **NCT07183189: SHR-A2009 plus aumolertinib vs aumolertinib, first line**: New open-label phase 3 registration by Suzhou Suncadia (n=582, active, not recruiting). The ADC SHR-A2009 plus aumolertinib is compared with aumolertinib monotherapy in treatment-naive EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. Registered 2025-09-19. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07183189
- **NCT07058519: ctDNA-guided adaptive osimertinib after osimertinib plus chemotherapy**: New adaptive phase 2 registration by Shanghai Chest Hospital (n=250, recruiting). Patients with EGFR-mutant ctDNA clearance after osimertinib plus chemotherapy receive osimertinib-based adaptive treatment guided by serial ctDNA monitoring. Endpoints are PFS in the adaptive period and time to ctDNA relapse. Registered 2025-07-10. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07058519
- **NCT06838273: BL-B01D1 plus osimertinib vs osimertinib, first line**: New open-label phase 3 registration by Sichuan Baili (n=720, recruiting). The ADC BL-B01D1 plus osimertinib is compared with osimertinib alone as first-line treatment of EGFR-mutant advanced NSCLC. Registered 2025-02-20. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06838273
- **NCT07155187: Telisotuzumab adizutecan alone or with osimertinib vs standard of care**: New phase 2/3 registration by AbbVie (n=490, recruiting). A phase 2 dose-selection stage of telisotuzumab adizutecan, alone or with osimertinib, is followed by a phase 3 comparison against standard of care at the recommended dose. Registered 2025-09-04. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07155187
- **NCT06956001: Firmonertinib vs platinum chemotherapy in EGFR PACC or L861Q mutations**: New open-label phase 3 registration by Allist (n=300, recruiting). First-line firmonertinib 240 mg daily is compared with platinum doublet chemotherapy, randomised 1:1 and stratified by mutation type and CNS metastasis. Registered 2025-05-02. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06956001
- **NCT07100080: Izalontamab brengitecan vs platinum-pemetrexed after EGFR-TKI failure**: New phase 2/3 registration by Bristol-Myers Squibb (n=500, recruiting). The ADC izalontamab brengitecan (BMS-986507) is compared with carboplatin/cisplatin plus pemetrexed in EGFR-mutant NSCLC after EGFR-TKI failure. Registered 2025-08-03. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07100080
- **NCT03521154: Osimertinib after chemoradiation in unresectable stage III EGFR-mutant NSCLC (results posted)**: Results were posted for AstraZeneca's global phase 3 trial (n=216, active, not recruiting) of osimertinib vs placebo after chemoradiation, a design matching LAURA. The record gives no numbers, so the posted results need to be read on the registry. Posted 2025-04-03. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT03521154
- **NCT04988295: Amivantamab-chemotherapy with or without lazertinib after osimertinib (results posted)**: Results were posted for Janssen's phase 3 trial (n=776, active, not recruiting), a design matching MARIPOSA-2. Arms are amivantamab plus carboplatin-pemetrexed with or without lazertinib vs carboplatin-pemetrexed in ex19del/L858R NSCLC after osimertinib failure. The record gives no numbers. Posted 2025-04-16. https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT04988295

### nsclc-resistance-nextgen

- **Genomic landscape of clinically acquired resistance alterations in patients treated with KRASG12C inhibitors.**: Post-progression cfDNA from 143 patients treated with KRAS G12C inhibitors (68 NSCLC, 58 CRC), with functional validation in cell models. Acquired RAS/MAPK alterations occurred in 46% overall but less often in NSCLC than CRC (26% vs 69%). Most alterations were confirmed as resistance drivers. RAS(ON) tri-complex inhibitors (RM-018, RMC-7797) remained active against them in vitro. Points to next-generation RAS inhibitors after G12C-inhibitor failure. The efficacy data are preclinical, and cfDNA may miss non-genetic resistance.
  Riedl JM, Fece de la Cruz F, Lin JJ et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39914665/
- **Trastuzumab rezetecan, a HER2-directed antibody-drug conjugate, in patients with advanced HER2-mutant non-small-cell lung cancer (HORIZON-Lung): phase 2 results from a multicentre, single-arm study.**: Single-arm phase 2 (n=94, China) of the HER2 ADC trastuzumab rezetecan (SHR-A1811) in pretreated HER2-mutant NSCLC. Confirmed ORR by independent review was 73%. Grade 3-4 haematological toxicity was common (neutropenia 40%), and treatment-related serious AEs occurred in 23%. A new HER2 ADC with high response rates. Single-arm, median follow-up only 8.7 months, and Chinese patients only.
  Li Z, Wang Y, Sun Y et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40020696/
- **Perioperative durvalumab plus chemotherapy plus new agents for resectable non-small-cell lung cancer: the platform phase 2 NeoCOAST-2 trial.**: Randomised phase 2 platform trial (n=202, 198 in mITT) in untreated resectable stage IIA-IIIB NSCLC. Neoadjuvant durvalumab was combined with chemotherapy plus oleclumab, chemotherapy plus monalizumab, or single-agent platinum plus datopotamab deruxtecan. pCR was 20.3%, 25.7% and 35.2%, and mPR was 41.9%, 50.0% and 63.0%, respectively. 93-94% of patients reached surgery, and the Dato-DXd arm had the lowest rate of grade 3 or higher treatment-related AEs (20.4%). This is the first neoadjuvant trial of a TROP2 ADC with chemo-immunotherapy. The endpoints are surrogate (pCR/mPR), there is no survival data, and the arms were not powered for comparison with each other.
  Cascone T, Bonanno L, Guisier F et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40450142/
- **A B7H3-targeting antibody-drug conjugate in advanced solid tumors: a phase 1/1b trial**: Global multicentre phase 1/1b dose-escalation and expansion trial (n=312) of YL201, a B7H3-targeting topoisomerase-1 ADC, in heavily pretreated advanced solid tumours. ORR was 63.9% in ES-SCLC and 28.6% in lung adenocarcinoma; grade >=3 toxicity was mainly haematological (neutropenia 31.7%), with ILD in 1.3%. It adds a new ADC option relevant to post-progression lung cancer, with phase 3 trials in SCLC under way. Limitations: non-randomised phase 1 data across mixed tumour types with small per-cohort samples, and B7H3 expression did not predict response.
  Ma Y, Yang Y, Huang Y et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40082695/
