- **Phase 2 Open-Label Study of Sacituzumab Govitecan as Second-Line Therapy in Patients With Extensive-Stage SCLC: Results From TROPiCS-03**: TROPiCS-03 ES-SCLC cohort: open-label phase 2 basket study of second-line sacituzumab govitecan after platinum and anti-PD-(L)1 therapy (n=43, median follow-up 12.3 months). Investigator-assessed ORR was 41.9%, with median DoR 4.73, PFS 4.40 and OS 13.60 months; activity was seen in both platinum-resistant (ORR 35.0%) and platinum-sensitive (47.8%) disease. It supports TROP-2 ADCs as a second-line SCLC option. Limitations: small single-arm cohort, short responses, grade >=3 TEAEs in 74.4%.
  Dowlati A, Chiang AC, Cervantes A et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39755168/
- **Vebreltinib in MET amplification-driven advanced non-small-cell lung cancer (KUNPENG): a single-arm, multi-cohort, multicentre, phase 2 study**: KUNPENG is a multicentre single-arm phase 2 trial of the selective MET inhibitor vebreltinib in MET amplification-driven (GCN >=6) advanced NSCLC in China; 86 MET-inhibitor-naive patients were analysed. ORR by masked independent review was 48.8% (95% CI 38.3-59.4) at median follow-up 18.6 months, with grade >=3 treatment-related AEs in 31% (mainly liver function). It provides prospective data for MET-amplified NSCLC, relevant to MET-directed strategies. Limitations: single-arm, all-Chinese and 90% male population, cohorts merged due to slow accrual, and de novo (not post-EGFR-TKI) MET amplification.
  Wu YL, Yao Y, Yang JJ et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41365311/
- **First-Line Sacituzumab Govitecan Plus Pembrolizumab in Metastatic NSCLC: PD-L1 TPS Less Than 50% and More Than or Equal to 50% Cohorts of the EVOKE-02 Study**: EVOKE-02 is a global open-label multicohort phase 2 study of first-line sacituzumab govitecan plus pembrolizumab in metastatic NSCLC without actionable alterations (n=92). ORR was 66.7% and median PFS 13.1 months in PD-L1 TPS >=50% (n=30), versus 29.0% and 7.0 months in TPS <50% (n=62); grade >=3 TEAEs occurred in 76.1% and 27.2% discontinued a study drug. It supports ongoing phase 3 testing of a TROP-2 ADC-IO first-line combination. Limitations: single-arm, small cohorts, no comparator, and patients with driver alterations (including EGFR) were excluded.
  Reck M, Patel JD, Gray JE et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41173143/
- **TROP-2-targeted antibody-drug conjugate SHR-A1921 for advanced or metastatic solid tumors: A first-in-human phase 1 study**: First-in-human three-stage phase 1 trial (n=391) of the TROP-2 ADC SHR-A1921 in pretreated advanced solid tumours, including NSCLC and SCLC cohorts. Overall ORR was 24.8% (18.2-43.1% across cohorts), grade >=3 treatment-related AEs occurred in 33.8% (mainly stomatitis) and grade >=3 neutropenia in only 3.1%; 3.0 mg/kg q3w was chosen for development. It adds another TROP-2 ADC to the post-resistance pipeline. Limitations: uncontrolled phase 1 across heterogeneous tumours, lung-specific results and EGFR-mutant subsets not given in the abstract, and TROP-2 expression did not predict efficacy.
  Zhong J, Wu L, Song Z et al. *Cancer Cell*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41135519/
- **Ifinatamab Deruxtecan in Patients With Extensive-Stage Small Cell Lung Cancer: Primary Analysis of the Phase II IDeate-Lung01 Trial**: Phase 2 IDeate-Lung01 trial (n=183) of the B7-H3 ADC ifinatamab deruxtecan in previously treated ES-SCLC, with randomised dose optimisation (8 vs 12 mg/kg). At 12 mg/kg (n=137), confirmed ORR was 48.2%, median DoR 5.3 months, median PFS 4.9 months and 9-month OS 59.1%; adjudicated ILD occurred in 12.4% (grade >=3 4.4%). It establishes I-DXd as a promising later-line SCLC option now being tested in phase 3 (IDeate-Lung02). Limitations: no comparator arm, short PFS and a notable ILD rate.
  Rudin CM, Johnson ML, Paz-Ares L et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41086386/
- **First-line sacituzumab tirumotecan with tagitanlimab in advanced non-small-cell lung cancer: a phase 2 trial**: Initial results of the ongoing non-randomised phase 2 OptiTROP-Lung01 study of first-line sacituzumab tirumotecan (TROP-2 ADC) plus the PD-L1 antibody tagitanlimab in advanced NSCLC without actionable alterations (cohort 1A n=40, cohort 1B n=63). Confirmed ORR was 40.0% and 66.7%, with median PFS 15.4 months in 1A and not reached in 1B; grade >=3 neutropenia occurred in 30-35% with no treatment-related deaths. It supports TROP-2 ADC plus immunotherapy as a first-line strategy under phase 3 evaluation. Limitations: not powered for hypothesis testing, no randomised comparator, different dosing schedules between cohorts, and EGFR/driver-positive patients excluded.
  Hong S, Wang Q, Cheng Y et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40830660/

### lung-top-journals

- **First-Line Camrelizumab Versus Placebo Plus Chemotherapy With or Without Radiotherapy for Brain Metastases in NSCLC: The CTONG 2003 Randomized Placebo-Controlled Trial.**: Double-blind randomised trial of camrelizumab vs placebo plus platinum chemotherapy (± brain RT) in EGFR/ALK-negative NSCLC with untreated brain metastases. It closed early after 60 of 200 planned patients. Intracranial PFS was 12.7 vs 9.9 months (HR 0.45, 95% CI 0.21-0.96), and PFS was 9.7 vs 6.7 months (HR 0.57, not significant). Grade >=3 TRAEs were 65.6% vs 46.4%. First randomised data on immunotherapy specifically for NSCLC brain metastases. Underpowered after early termination, and it excludes EGFR-mutant patients.
  Li YS, Yu Q, Bu Q et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39929333/
- **Efficacy of Zenocutuzumab in NRG1 Fusion-Positive Cancer.**: Registrational phase 2 study (n=204, 12 tumour types) of the HER2xHER3 bispecific zenocutuzumab in NRG1 fusion-positive cancers. ORR was 30% overall and 29% (27/93) in NSCLC, with median DOR 11.1 months and PFS 6.8 months; adverse events were mostly grade 1-2. Establishes a targeted option for NRG1-fusion NSCLC. Single-arm, and response rates are moderate.
  Schram AM, Goto K, Kim DW et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39908431/
- **A Single-Arm Phase 2 Study of Sotorasib Plus Carboplatin and Pemetrexed in Patients With Advanced Nonsquamous NSCLC With KRAS G12C Mutation (WJOG14821L, SCARLET).**: Single-arm phase 2 (n=30, Japan) of first-line sotorasib plus carboplatin-pemetrexed in KRAS G12C nonsquamous NSCLC. ORR was 88.9%, median PFS 6.6 months and OS 20.6 months; early ctDNA clearance was associated with longer PFS. Supports chemotherapy-KRAS inhibitor combinations, notably in PD-L1-negative disease. The high ORR contrasts with a short PFS, and the cohort is very small.
  Akamatsu H, Sakata S, Azuma K et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39828218/
- **Brain radiotherapy combined with camrelizumab and platinum-doublet chemotherapy for previously untreated advanced non-small-cell lung cancer with brain metastases (C-Brain): a multicentre, single-arm, phase 2 trial.**: Single-arm phase 2 (n=65, nine Chinese centres) of brain RT (SRS or WBRT) plus camrelizumab and platinum doublet in driver-negative NSCLC with new brain metastases. The 6-month PFS rate was 71.7% at a median follow-up of 14.1 months. Supports combining brain radiotherapy with chemo-immunotherapy up front. Single-arm, mixed RT modalities, 92% male, and not applicable to EGFR-mutant disease.
  Xu Y, Chen K, Xu Y et al. *Lancet Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39756446/
- **Subcutaneous versus intravenous pembrolizumab, in combination with chemotherapy, for treatment of metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer: the phase III 3475A-D77 trial.**: Phase 3 randomised 2:1 PK non-inferiority trial (n=377) of subcutaneous vs IV pembrolizumab with chemotherapy in driver-negative metastatic NSCLC. Exposure was non-inferior (AUC GMR 1.14; Ctrough GMR 1.67), and ORR was similar (45.4% vs 42.1%), with a median injection time of 2 minutes. Supports subcutaneous pembrolizumab as an alternative route. The primary endpoints are pharmacokinetic; efficacy is secondary and follow-up short (9.6 months).
  Felip E, Rojas CI, Schenker M et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40157574/
- **Cemiplimab Monotherapy for First-Line Treatment of Patients with Advanced NSCLC With PD-L1 Expression of 50% or Higher: Five-Year Outcomes of EMPOWER-Lung 1.**: Five-year update of the phase 3 EMPOWER-Lung 1 trial (n=712) of first-line cemiplimab vs chemotherapy in PD-L1 >=50% NSCLC without EGFR/ALK/ROS1 alterations. In verified PD-L1 >=50% patients (n=565), median OS was 26.1 vs 13.3 months (HR 0.59), and 5-year OS 29.0% vs 15.0%. Grade >=3 TRAEs were 18.3% vs 39.9%. Confirms durable benefit of single-agent PD-1 blockade. Not applicable to EGFR-mutant disease, and the abstract reports no subgroup beyond PD-L1 level and histology.
  Kilickap S, Baramidze A, Sezer A et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40118215/
- **Ivonescimab versus pembrolizumab for PD-L1-positive non-small cell lung cancer (HARMONi-2): a randomised, double-blind, phase 3 study in China.**: Double-blind phase 3 (n=398, China) of the PD-1/VEGF bispecific ivonescimab vs pembrolizumab first line in PD-L1-positive NSCLC without EGFR/ALK alterations. At interim analysis, median PFS was 11.1 vs 5.8 months (HR 0.51), consistent across PD-L1 strata. Grade >=3 TRAEs were 29% vs 16%. First phase 3 win of a bispecific over pembrolizumab monotherapy. Interim PFS only with OS immature, a Chinese-only population, and EGFR-mutant patients excluded.
  Xiong A, Wang L, Chen J et al. *Lancet*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40057343/
- **HER2-Selective Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitor, Zongertinib (BI 1810631), in Patients With Advanced/Metastatic Solid Tumors With HER2 Alterations: A Phase Ia Dose-Escalation Study.**: Phase Ia dose escalation (n=105) of the EGFR-sparing HER2-selective TKI zongertinib in HER2-altered solid tumours. MTD was not reached, and grade >=3 TRAEs occurred in 10%. In 54 NSCLC patients, confirmed ORR was 35%, including 28% after prior HER2-directed therapy; median PFS with once-daily dosing was 17.2 months. A new oral HER2 option for HER2-mutant NSCLC. Dose-escalation data in mixed tumours at varied doses, so efficacy estimates are preliminary.
  Heymach JV, Opdam F, Barve M et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40030100/
- **Clonal driver neoantigen loss under EGFR TKI and immune selection pressures.**: Single-patient phylogenetic study of an EGFR ex19del lung cancer treated with erlotinib, osimertinib, radiotherapy and a personalised neopeptide vaccine, with ctDNA tracking of 467 variants. A pre-existing EGFR-wild-type, small-cell-transformed clone expanded during osimertinib/vaccine therapy and lost vaccine-targeted neoantigens through chromosomal instability despite systemic T-cell reactivity to the ex19del neoantigen; TRACERx 421 data suggest pre-whole-genome-doubling neoantigens are more stable vaccine targets. Relevant to neoantigen-vaccine design and resistance in EGFR-mutant disease, but rests on one patient (n=1) plus cohort-level inference.
  Al Bakir M, Reading JL, Gamble S et al. *Nature*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39972134/
- **Atezolizumab plus bevacizumab and chemotherapy in metastatic nonsquamous NSCLC: the randomized double-blind phase 3 IMpower151 trial.**: Randomised, double-blind phase 3 trial in China (n=305; 53% EGFR+) of atezolizumab plus bevacizumab and chemotherapy versus placebo plus bevacizumab and chemotherapy as first-line treatment for nonsquamous NSCLC. The trial missed its primary endpoint: median INV-PFS 9.5 vs 7.1 months (HR 0.84, 95% CI 0.65-1.09; P=0.184), median OS 20.7 vs 18.7 months (HR 0.93), ORR 48% vs 50%. A negative phase 3 result that does not support adding atezolizumab in this largely EGFR-positive Chinese population; the abstract gives no separate EGFR-subgroup numbers.
  Zhou C, Dong X, Chen G et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40379995/
- **Zongertinib in Previously Treated HER2-Mutant Non-Small-Cell Lung Cancer.**: Multicohort phase 1a-1b trial of the oral HER2-selective TKI zongertinib in previously treated HER2-mutant NSCLC. In cohort 1 (tyrosine-kinase-domain mutations, 120 mg, n=75) confirmed ORR was 71% (95% CI 60-80), median DoR 14.1 months and median PFS 12.4 months, with grade 3+ drug-related AEs in 17%; ORR was 48% after a prior HER2 ADC (n=31) and 30% for non-TKD mutations (n=20), and no drug-related ILD was seen. Supports zongertinib as a targeted option for HER2-mutant NSCLC (not EGFR-directed), but data come from single-arm early-phase cohorts without a comparator and with short follow-up.
  Heymach JV, Ruiter G, Ahn MJ et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40293180/
- **Patritumab Deruxtecan (HER3-DXd; MK-1022) in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer After Platinum-Based Chemotherapy and Immunotherapy.**: Single-arm trial cohort (n=47) of the HER3-directed ADC patritumab deruxtecan (5.6 mg/kg every 3 weeks) in advanced NSCLC without common EGFR-activating mutations, after platinum chemotherapy, immunotherapy and targeted therapy where relevant. Confirmed ORR was 27.7%, median DoR 8.1 months, median PFS 5.5 months and median OS 15.2 months; grade 3+ drug-related TEAEs occurred in 51.1% and adjudicated ILD in 10.6% (all grade 1-2). Extends HER3-DXd activity beyond EGFR-mutant NSCLC; the cohort is small and single-arm, and the abstract does not name the trial phase.
  Steuer CE, Hayashi H, Su WC et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40554742/
- **Perioperative tislelizumab plus neoadjuvant chemotherapy for patients with resectable non-small-cell lung cancer: final analysis of the randomized RATIONALE-315 trial**: Final analysis of RATIONALE-315, a randomised double-blind phase 3 trial in China (n=453) of perioperative tislelizumab plus neoadjuvant chemotherapy versus neoadjuvant chemotherapy plus placebo in resectable stage II-IIIA NSCLC. At median follow-up 38.5 months, OS improved significantly (HR 0.65, 95% CI 0.45-0.93; 36-month OS 79.3% vs 69.3%) and EFS was improved (HR 0.58; median not reached vs 30.6 months). It adds an OS result to support perioperative chemoimmunotherapy as standard of care. Limitations: Chinese population only, median OS not reached in either arm, and EGFR-mutant patients are not addressed in the abstract.
  Wang C, Wang W, Liu H et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41344593/
- **Longitudinal ultrasensitive ctDNA monitoring for high-resolution lung cancer risk prediction**: TRACERx analysis applying whole-genome, tumour-informed ultrasensitive ctDNA detection (1,800 variants; 2,994 plasma samples) in 431 patients with resected NSCLC. ctDNA detection below 80 parts per million pre- and postoperatively was highly prognostic, combined pre/post status defined an intermediate-risk group, and patients who cleared ctDNA during adjuvant therapy had better outcomes. It supports ctDNA/MRD-guided stratification of adjuvant therapy in early NSCLC. Limitations: observational prognostic cohort, no randomised test of ctDNA-guided treatment, and effect sizes are not given in the abstract.
  Black JRM, Karasaki T, Abbott CW et al. *Cell*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41205598/
- **Ivonescimab plus chemotherapy versus tislelizumab plus chemotherapy as first-line treatment for advanced squamous non-small-cell lung cancer (HARMONi-6): a randomised, double-blind, phase 3 trial**: HARMONi-6 is a randomised double-blind phase 3 trial at 50 sites in China (n=532) of first-line ivonescimab (PD-1/VEGF bispecific) plus paclitaxel-carboplatin versus tislelizumab plus the same chemotherapy in advanced squamous NSCLC. Median PFS was 11.1 vs 6.9 months (HR 0.60, 95% CI 0.46-0.78), consistent across PD-L1 subgroups. It is the first phase 3 win for a PD-1/VEGF bispecific over an active PD-1 chemo-IO comparator in squamous disease. Limitations: short median follow-up (10.3 months), OS immature, China-only population.
  Chen Z, Yang F, Jiang Z et al. *Lancet*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41125109/
- **Alectinib versus crizotinib in previously untreated ALK-positive advanced non-small cell lung cancer: final overall survival analysis of the phase III ALEX study**: Final analysis of the global randomised phase 3 ALEX trial (n=303) of first-line alectinib versus crizotinib in ALK-positive advanced NSCLC. Median OS was 81.1 vs 54.2 months (HR 0.78, 95% CI 0.56-1.08), with benefit in patients with baseline CNS metastases (63.4 vs 30.9 months) and much longer DoR (42.3 vs 11.1 months). It confirms long-term systemic and intracranial benefit of alectinib in a driver subgroup. Limitations: OS difference not statistically significant (CI crosses 1), confounded by crossover/subsequent therapies, and newer comparators (lorlatinib) are not addressed.
  Peters S, Camidge R, Dziadziuszko R et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2026. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41110693/
- **Updated Overall Survival Analysis From the Phase II PHAROS Study of Encorafenib Plus Binimetinib in Patients With BRAF V600E-Mutant Metastatic Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer**: Updated analysis of the single-arm phase 2 PHAROS study (n=98; 59 treatment-naive, 39 pretreated) of encorafenib plus binimetinib in BRAF V600E-mutant metastatic NSCLC. Median OS was 47.6 months (4-year OS 49%) in treatment-naive and 22.7 months (4-year OS 31%) in pretreated patients after about 4 years of follow-up, with consistent safety. It provides mature survival data for a targeted option in this driver subgroup. Limitations: single-arm, modest sample, cross-trial comparisons only.
  Johnson ML, Smit EF, Felip E et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41109959/
- **Simultaneous Durvalumab and Platinum-Based Chemoradiotherapy in Unresectable Stage III Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: The Phase III PACIFIC-2 Study**: PACIFIC-2 is a randomised (2:1) placebo-controlled phase 3 trial (n=328) of durvalumab started concurrently with platinum-based chemoradiotherapy and continued as consolidation in unresectable stage III NSCLC. There was no significant difference in PFS (HR 0.85, 95% CI 0.65-1.12) or OS (HR 1.03; OS24 58.4% vs 59.5%), with more discontinuations (25.6% vs 12.0%) and fatal AEs (13.7% vs 10.2%) on durvalumab. It is a negative trial confirming that consolidation durvalumab after cCRT remains standard. Limitations: control arm lacked consolidation immunotherapy, so it does not test concurrent versus sequential durvalumab directly.
  Bradley JD, Sugawara S, Lee KH et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41082707/
- **Early and locally advanced non-small-cell lung cancer: ESMO Clinical Practice Guideline for diagnosis, treatment and follow-up**: Updated ESMO Clinical Practice Guideline covering diagnosis, treatment and follow-up of early-stage and locally advanced NSCLC (no abstract available). As a major European guideline, it sets reference standards for perioperative, adjuvant and stage III management, including targeted and immunotherapy approaches in driver subgroups such as EGFR-mutant disease. The specific recommendations and evidence levels are not available from the record and should be checked in the full text.
  Zer A, Ahn MJ, Barlesi F et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40885528/
- **Hippocampal Avoidance During Prophylactic Cranial Irradiation for Patients With Small Cell Lung Cancer: Randomized Phase II/III Trial NRG-CC003**: NRG-CC003 is a randomised phase II/III trial (n=393) of prophylactic cranial irradiation with versus without hippocampal avoidance in SCLC without brain metastases. HA-PCI met noninferiority for 12-month intracranial relapse (14.7% vs 14.8%) and reduced failure on any neurocognitive test (adjusted HR 0.78, 95% CI 0.61-0.99), but did not meet the primary phase III endpoint of 6-month delayed-recall preservation (25.5% vs 30.0%, P=.28); OS and grade >=3 toxicity were similar. It supports hippocampal avoidance when PCI is used in SCLC. Limitations: primary neurocognitive endpoint not met, and the role of PCI versus MRI surveillance is not addressed.
  Gondi V, Pugh SL, Mehta MP et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40789106/
- **Adagrasib versus docetaxel in KRASG12C-mutated non-small-cell lung cancer (KRYSTAL-12): a randomised, open-label, phase 3 trial**: KRYSTAL-12 is a randomised open-label phase 3 trial at 230 centres (n=453, 2:1) of adagrasib versus docetaxel in KRAS G12C-mutated NSCLC after platinum chemotherapy and anti-PD-(L)1 therapy. Median PFS by blinded central review was 5.5 vs 3.8 months (HR 0.58, 95% CI 0.45-0.76); grade >=3 treatment-related AEs occurred in 47% vs 46%. It confirms a PFS benefit for a KRAS G12C inhibitor over docetaxel in a driver subgroup. Limitations: short median follow-up (7.2 months), open-label design, and OS data not reported in the abstract.
  Barlesi F, Yao W, Duruisseaux M et al. *Lancet*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40783289/
- **Phase III Study of Mediastinal Lymph Node Dissection for Ground Glass Opacity-Dominant Lung Adenocarcinoma**: ECTOP-1009 is a multicentre open-label phase 3 noninferiority RCT comparing no mediastinal lymph node dissection with systematic LND in GGO-dominant invasive lung adenocarcinoma predicted to be node-negative. At the planned interim analysis (n=302), no nodal metastases were found in either arm, and omitting LND shortened surgery (74 vs 109 min), reduced blood loss and hospital stay, while the LND arm had two serious complications; the trial was terminated on nonmaleficence grounds. It supports omitting systematic mediastinal LND in this selected group. Limitations: interim analysis without mature 3-year DFS (the primary endpoint), single-country population, and strict selection criteria.
  Zhang Y, Qian B, Song Q et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40690727/
- **Therapy for Stage IV Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer With Driver Alterations: ASCO Living Guideline, Version 2025.1**: ASCO living guideline (version 2025.1) on therapy for stage IV NSCLC with driver alterations, including EGFR, updated by a standing expert panel through continuous systematic review. As a major guideline it sets the US reference for first-line and subsequent targeted therapy in driver subgroups. The abstract gives only methodology; the specific recommendation changes in this version must be read in the full text.
  Reuss JE, Kuruvilla S, Ismaila N et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40674661/
- **Adjuvant chemotherapy for stage IA-IIA non-squamous, non-small-cell lung cancer identified as molecular high-risk by a 14-gene expression profile (AIM-HIGH): an international, randomised, phase 3 trial**: AIM-HIGH is an international randomised phase 3 trial (45 centres in France, Germany and the USA) of platinum-based adjuvant chemotherapy versus observation in stage IA-IIA non-squamous NSCLC classed as molecular high-risk by a 14-gene expression assay; 236 of 449 stratified patients were randomised. This record reports the prespecified interim analysis of 24-month DFS, with 87 and 107 evaluable patients, but the abstract is cut off before the efficacy results, so no effect estimates can be given here. It tests a molecular-risk-guided adjuvant strategy for early-stage disease. Limitations: interim analysis, small evaluable mITT population, and results must be checked in the full text.
  Spigel DR, Westeel V, Anderson IC et al. *Lancet Respir Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40578381/
- **Updated treatment recommendations for systemic treatment: from the ESMO oncogene-addicted metastatic NSCLC living guideline**: ESMO living guideline letter giving updated systemic treatment recommendations for oncogene-addicted metastatic NSCLC, the category that includes EGFR-mutant disease. As a major European guideline update it is directly relevant to first-line and post-progression choices in EGFR-mutant NSCLC. No abstract is available, so the specific changes must be read in the full text.
  Hendriks LEL, Cortiula F, Martins-Branco D et al. *Ann Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40571160/
- **Efficacy and safety of first-line maintenance therapy with lurbinectedin plus atezolizumab in extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer (IMforte): a randomised, multicentre, open-label, phase 3 trial**: IMforte is a randomised open-label phase 3 trial in 13 countries: after atezolizumab-carboplatin-etoposide induction, 483 patients with ES-SCLC without progression were randomised to maintenance lurbinectedin plus atezolizumab or atezolizumab alone. Both independently assessed PFS (HR 0.54, 95% CI 0.43-0.67) and OS (HR 0.73, 95% CI 0.57-0.95) favoured the combination. It is the first positive phase 3 trial of an intensified first-line maintenance strategy in ES-SCLC. Limitations: open-label, only patients without progression after induction were randomised (27% of enrolled did not reach maintenance), and toxicity details and medians are cut from the abstract.
  Paz-Ares L, Borghaei H, Liu SV et al. *Lancet*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40473449/
- **Tarlatamab in Small-Cell Lung Cancer after Platinum-Based Chemotherapy**: DeLLphi-304 is a multinational open-label phase 3 trial (n=509) of the DLL3 bispecific T-cell engager tarlatamab versus investigator's choice chemotherapy (topotecan, lurbinectedin or amrubicin) as second-line therapy in SCLC. At the prespecified interim analysis, median OS was 13.6 vs 8.3 months (HR 0.60, 95% CI 0.47-0.77), PFS and dyspnoea/cough also improved, and grade >=3 AEs were less frequent (54% vs 80%). It establishes tarlatamab as a new second-line standard in SCLC. Limitations: open-label design and interim analysis.
  Mountzios G, Sun L, Cho BC et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40454646/
- **Overall Survival with Neoadjuvant Nivolumab plus Chemotherapy in Lung Cancer**: Planned OS analysis of the open-label phase 3 CheckMate 816 trial (n=358) of three cycles of neoadjuvant nivolumab plus chemotherapy versus chemotherapy alone in resectable stage IB-IIIA NSCLC. OS was significantly improved (HR 0.72, 95% CI 0.523-0.998; 5-year OS 65.4% vs 55.0%) at median follow-up 68.4 months; exploratory analyses showed 5-year OS of 95.3% with pCR and 75.0% with presurgical ctDNA clearance. It confirms an OS benefit for neoadjuvant chemoimmunotherapy and supports pCR/ctDNA as outcome markers. Limitations: P value just under 0.05 with CI upper bound near 1, open-label design, and pCR/ctDNA analyses are exploratory.
  Forde PM, Spicer JD, Provencio M et al. *N Engl J Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40454642/
- **Activating Mutations in the MET Kinase Domain Co-Occur With Other Driver Oncogenes and Mediate Resistance to Targeted Therapy in NSCLC**: Genomic study of more than 115,000 NSCLC tumours from two cohorts plus preclinical models of activating MET tyrosine kinase domain (TKD) mutations. MET TKD mutations occurred in 0.15% of cases, about half co-occurring with another oncogenic driver; in 8 cases with sequential testing the mutation was acquired after systemic therapy in 88%, possibly through APOBEC mutagenesis, and in vitro it conferred resistance to targeted therapy that was overcome by combined inhibition of the primary oncogene and MET. It identifies an off-target resistance mechanism with a direct treatment implication (adding MET inhibition) for oncogene-driven NSCLC, including EGFR-mutant disease. Limitations: rare alteration, very small sequential-testing series, and combination benefit shown only in vitro.
  Nakazawa S, Pecci F, Ricciuti B et al. *J Thorac Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40185329/
- **Taletrectinib in ROS1+ Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: TRUST**: Pooled analysis of the single-arm registrational phase 2 TRUST-I and TRUST-II trials of the CNS-active ROS1 TKI taletrectinib in ROS1+ NSCLC (efficacy n=273, safety n=352). Confirmed ORR was 88.8% (intracranial 76.5%) with median PFS 45.6 months in TKI-naive patients and 55.8% (intracranial 65.6%) with PFS 9.7 months in TKI-pretreated patients; ORR was 61.5% (8/13) with G2032R. It adds a CNS-active option for a driver subgroup, including after prior TKI. Limitations: non-randomised, cross-trial pooling, and frequent (mostly grade 1) GI and liver enzyme AEs.
  Pérol M, Li W, Pennell NA et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40179330/
- **Cerebrospinal fluid circulating tumor DNA profiling for risk stratification and matched treatment of central nervous system metastases**: Cohort study of 584 patients with NSCLC and CNS metastases with comprehensive CSF ctDNA profiling, with a prognostic model validated in an independent cohort. CSF ctDNA positivity predicted shorter survival (HR 1.9, 95% CI 1.56-2.39), patients with a CSF-detected driver given matched therapy had better survival (HR 0.78), and longitudinal CSF monitoring identified CNS-specific resistance mechanisms where a second matched therapy was associated with improved survival (HR 0.56). It supports CSF liquid biopsy to guide therapy for CNS disease, including in EGFR-mutant NSCLC. Limitations: observational and largely retrospective treatment comparisons with potential selection bias.
  Zheng MM, Zhou Q, Chen HJ et al. *Nat Med*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40016451/
- **Selpercatinib in RET Fusion-Positive Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer: Final Safety and Efficacy, Including Overall Survival, From the LIBRETTO-001 Phase I/II Trial**: Final analysis of the single-arm registrational phase I/II LIBRETTO-001 trial of selpercatinib in RET fusion-positive NSCLC (247 platinum-pretreated, 69 treatment-naive). ORR was 62% and 83%, median PFS 26.2 and 22.0 months, and median OS 47.6 months (pretreated) and not reached (naive), with 3-year OS 57% and 66%; CNS-ORR was 85% in 26 patients with measurable brain metastases. It provides mature survival and CNS data for a driver subgroup. Limitations: single-arm design, small treatment-naive and CNS cohorts.
  Gautschi O, Park K, Solomon BJ et al. *J Clin Oncol*, 2025. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39983053/

## Medium

- Comparing the Effectiveness and Safety of First-line Interventions in Patients With Advanced Epidermal Growth Factor Receptor-mutant Non-small Cell Lung Cancer, With Particular Focus on Brain Metastatic Status: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis. (Clin Oncol (R Coll Radiol)). Network meta-analysis of first-line RCTs in EGFR-mutant NSCLC stratified by brain metastasis status. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39951884/
- Rational Design of a Potent, Selective, and Metabolically Stable CDK9 Inhibitor to Counteract Osimertinib Resistance through Mcl-1 Suppression and Enhanced BRD4 Co-Targeting. (J Med Chem). Preclinical CDK9 inhibitor designed to counter osimertinib resistance via Mcl-1 suppression. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39947929/
- Detection of Overlooked Rare EGFR Mutations in Non-small Cell Lung Cancer Using Multigene Testing. (Thorac Cancer). 418-patient study showing a common multigene panel can miss druggable rare EGFR mutations. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39947926/
- Comparative safety profile of tyrosine kinase inhibitors in NSCLC: a network meta-analysis of hypertension and thrombotic risks. (Front Pharmacol). Network meta-analysis of RCTs comparing hypertension and thrombotic risk across NSCLC TKIs. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39944617/
- Longitudinal recurrence risk of adjuvant cytotoxic chemotherapy and gefitnib in resected lung cancer: A combined analysis of phase III studies. (Lung Cancer). Pooled analysis of two phase 3 adjuvant trials (JIPANG, IMPACT) on recurrence timing by EGFR status and treatment. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39938189/
- EGFR testing practices, treatment choice and clinical outcomes in advanced NSCLC in a real-world setting: A retrospective analysis of a US-based electronic health records database. (Lung Cancer). Large US EHR real-world study of EGFR testing, treatment choice and outcomes. https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39933217/
- Comprehensive molecular profiling of squamous non-small cell lung cancer reveals high incidence of actionable genomic alterations among patients with no history of smoking. (Lung Cancer).
